# CHARACTER STATE — Volume 02, Batch 0003 (Chapters 71–80), as of Sunday 28 February 2025, about 23:30

## Elias Venn, 32

**Barred from the Museum since 1 December 2024 as a security matter, save attendance at a hearing or inquiry at which he is a party or a witness, on notice, in company, not before eight. No post, no access, no employer, no card, no key, a pound, nine pages, a plastic wallet with four things in it, and a municipal remittance on the first of the month at cost centre M.Q.S. 4 for a post that no longer exists. He is on a payroll and the payroll is the reason he is a legal neighbour, and he has worked that out and told nobody.**

**What changed in this batch, and it is not small and it is not a power:**
- **He was in a room in Northglass on 6 February because a piece of paper had his name on it, and that is the first room in this case he has walked into rather than been let into.** He noticed it on the stair and made himself stop noticing it.
- **He conceded the fifth of December out loud, in a room, in nine seconds, and it cost him the last thing he had that was his.** His own words: *it is the only date in this case with a person in it, and I have been looking for a person since the sixth of December because I did not want the case to be a machine.* Ivo Pell was kind about it and he had to go and stand by a window.
- **The plastic wallet came out on a table in a room for the first time in nine weeks and went back in, and he keeps it, and it is worth nothing, and the Director told him in a corridor on the fourth floor at about half past four on Friday 5 February that she would not take it off him, and he believed her only on 12 February.**
- **He said his own rule out loud, to a stranger, at a door, for the first time in his life**, and told her why, and told her what it costs, which is that in about a month he will not be able to say that he went.
- **He refused a word, for the fourth time in twelve weeks, and was told by the man who keeps the practice that a street does not own it either, because a street is a council with a different name.** He did not want it and it cost him something and it is on the page.
- **He was asked a question by somebody outside this building for the first time in twelve weeks and answered it: yes, he writes it down, nine pages, thirty-five pence, in a coat, no copy, not filed, deliberately, under clause six.** And he was told three things back, the third of which is the most important sentence anybody has said to him in this case: *if your evidence cannot be produced then it cannot be tested. An untestable witness in a listed hearing is not a protection. It is a hole in the case with a person standing in it.*
- **He was told not to answer a question in a hall on 28 February, and he did not answer it, and the not-answering is the first correct thing he has done in a room since 6 December.** He was also told not to say he is not allowed into the building, because that is the easiest sentence anybody has said to him in twelve weeks and it is worth nothing.
- **He has stopped trying to be the person who answers the volume's question.** He wrote it down on 20 February: *I have decided to stop being the one who finds it, and that is the second thing I have given up in eight days and I have not lost anything by either of them.*
- **He read nothing in three weeks and a day.** Five times, and it is a method and not a coincidence, and the reason is in a four-line minute with two names on it and neither of them his.
- **The pound went on a return fare to Pemberton Row on Friday 26 February and he has not told anybody and it is in the notebook, which is the correct place for it.**

**He has no stop word and nobody who had the standing to offer him one did. He has refused four things in twelve weeks — a word, a piece of paper, a hand out of a file, and a rule that belongs to a street. The reason for all four is the three lines at the top of the first page of a notebook that cost thirty-five pence.**

**His new question, and it is not the old one: *who keeps it*.** Not where. Who. And it is a question about a person and the first one in the case that is.

## Mara Iles, 28

**Senior Conservator and Accession Registrar, five years in this building. `I do not know what you are for` stands and has not been taken back and nobody is to take it back.** In this batch she:
- **got the answer she asked for on 22 January and minuted that she had got it**, and read her own letter into box nine of Form A/3, *supplementary statement by another person in the matter*, and said in a committee room that it was of no use otherwise, because it came from her and not from him and if it did not it was nothing.
- **took the cost of the Museum's two decisions out loud, in order, twice**, and minuted that the sixth of March is a date on a question that cannot be asked and that they are going anyway, *because in about nine weeks somebody is going to describe this case as a campaign and it is not a campaign, it is two people going to a building to be told no properly by a woman who is right.*
- **said the other half of the practice's impossibility, and it is the reason the practice is not a programme: rule three is a permission, and a rota is a duty, and a duty a person can stop at any point without a form is a suggestion with a list on it.** She is the one who said *the Museum can hold the mat and cannot hold the knock*, and *a knock is not an object and there is no accession number for one and there is no box in this country, and the only reason there has not been one in four hundred years is that nobody has ever needed one, and about nine people in Low Ward do.*
- **refused, in that hall, in February, to say whether the practice is a thing or a place**, and gave the reason, and it is the volume's discipline stated by a professional: *if it turns out to be the object then this hall is furniture and there are nine mats in this city, and if it turns out to be the place then this is a street with a landlord and a cycle date and a document, and a thing with a document in it is a programme.*
- **stopped anybody asking Frank Dunning whether he was at home on the fifth of December**, and gave the reason in eleven seconds, and it was arithmetic and not kindness: the only word wanted from him is *no*, and a *no* about a Saturday afternoon is not about a field, and after that he is a man in a hearing who said one thing once.
- **said the sentence that keeps the whole of this out of a god**, in a store on the ground floor, at the back behind the loading bay, on 25 February, and asked for it to be minuted somewhere: a clerk of works answered one question and it is the right answer to that one, and it is not the answer to what a cord line is doing in a room with nine small things in it that people stop at, and no professional is going to answer that in a store on a Thursday morning, and she would like whoever reads it in four years to know that a professional looked at it and did not claim it.
- **wrote four lines on a man's sheet in her own hand, with his permission, promising to say nothing**, to be read on his step on 22 March, and said it is the first thing she has ever written that is a promise to say nothing, and asked him to decide whether it is handed out.
- **defended a dead man's name in a schedule of collections to a trustee of about seventy**, said the cost out loud before the opinion, said *there is no field on any form in this building for a person's name in a description of a place and there has not been one since about 1860*, and wrote it because the schedule is the only index that cannot be lost. **She is frightened of it and she did it.**

## Ivo Pell, 25

**Systems technician, four years in post, the Museum's named subscriber contact.** In this batch he:
- **was the only person in the room who could identify the subscriber, and did so before anything about a file was said**, and put *there is a box eleven* into a Registry intake form and was right.
- **asked what a date of permanence is at half past eleven on a Wednesday morning and got the batch's hinge: a door mat has none because you can buy another one for two pounds, and a house has one because somebody wrote a piece of paper about when the term ends.** And then: *the Museum's block is not a list, it is a filter, and the selection criterion is geography, and a tenancy is the only document in this country that says a person is a neighbour of a place.*
- **said the thing about the fifth of December that nobody wanted to say** — eighty-eight is a Saturday problem, the tenancy is current, nobody has ever served a notice on it, and a walk survey means a man at a door and a man of about sixty-five was out — and then said it in about nine seconds, standing up, because a man of thirty-two had just handed him a nine-week mistake and somebody had to.
- **classified the nine blanks: four in the programme, one a Saturday afternoon, five with no date anywhere, and a programme cannot explain a row that cannot be placed on a day** — and said the five are the ones to be frightened of, which is the first time in nine weeks he has wanted a smaller number than the other one.
- **was right about the rope in January, and was not in the room on 25 February, and was not asked**, and a man in a coat wrote it down in his favour and against nobody.
- **asked where a practice has to happen for it to be the same thing, on 20 February, in a hall, badly, on purpose**, and nobody could answer it, and he said he was not going to be the one who picks.

## Anja Ward, 53

**Director, nineteen years. She says the cost out loud before she decides, in a room, and it is minuted in that order, and in this batch she said it four times and was right four times.** In this batch she:
- **discovered in a corridor on 10 February that she has been Director for nineteen years and does not know who owns the freehold of a street she has written about four hundred letters to**, and ordered a written search, and said it took her about four seconds.
- **wrote to a letting agent asking a question and not a document, and said the cost out loud first, twice, and the second cost was that she was about to make a document about a number of addresses and could not see how to do that without making a list of people, and signed it anyway and asked that it be minuted that nobody stopped her.**
- **held a public meeting in a public gallery for the second time in this case, about a hundred and ten people, and opened it by saying that this Museum is the last institution in the chain and not the first and has behaved for ten weeks as though it were in the middle of it**, and that the furthest downstream of everything is a woman in a shop in Fisher Lane who has sold nine mats since 4 January and does not know it.
- **was told by a solicitor that her meeting is how a commercial programme left a museum, and said thank you, and did not argue.**
- **declined, in a room of about a hundred and ten people, to name a household that is owed nothing and is on no list, on the ground that a name of a person in a public room is a hook** — and said she had wanted the sentence and had looked for a reason to say it and had not found one. She also said the Director's letter of 11 February cost her the sentence *I have spent nineteen years not having a sentence about me in this building.*
- **said out loud, in public, that she has failed for ten weeks to get the Registry to answer the question she went there to ask, and that a Director who is still trying in March is going to cost somebody another fortnight.** That is the first time a Director of this building has said a thing like that in a public room.

## Sonia Boulton, about thirty-nine, MRICS — letting agent, Morton Hay & Baker (Letting & Management) Ltd, 11 Pemberton Row — NEW IN THIS BATCH

**Eleven years in post. Manages about four hundred and twelve tenancies in the ward for one freeholder. Has a hardback book: every visit she has made since about 2019, in her hand, with a date. It is the only register in this case and she will not give it to anybody, and she said the same sentence about it as about the schedule, and she meant it about both.**

**She is the competent professional at the centre of this batch and she is not a villain and she has not done anything wrong and she is not a fool.** She **refused six things since the autumn** — a council's estate team, a ward councillor, an insurance broker, a journalist, a competitor, and a man in a coat through a records clerk in four seconds — **and the seventh is the Museum.** Her reasons are professional and she gives them before she is asked: a schedule of current tenancies is not a record, it is the live rent roll, and if it goes into a museum it will be in a museum in four years when there is a question about a person at an address, and the person who goes to it will be somebody doing a correct thing inside a department.

**What she did:** she came to a museum on a Thursday afternoon because a Director asked a question in writing with a purpose on it; **she refused the rent roll and answered the question by reading the programme out loud in a committee room in about nineteen minutes, with no tenancies and no names in it, and said the room could not write it down and that was the point and that it was her professional opinion about the Museum;** she read the sentence of a tenancy being the only document that says a person is a neighbour of a place; she answered a man of about forty in a public gallery in nine seconds when he stood up and asked what he was meant to say; **she signed a Statement of Tenure Programme that is the first good instrument in twelve weeks, and she was told by a Director that it would be described in no other terms than an answer to a question, and she accepted that in writing.**

**What she said that is the batch's finding, twice, and to two audiences, and neither time softened:** *in about four months' time nobody will be able to tell the difference between a house that has been emptied on purpose and a house where somebody did not answer the door on a Tuesday afternoon. And when that is true then it will not be a machine that has done it. It will be that nobody can ask.* And: *it is not your homes. It is the ability to be recognised as a legal neighbour, and that is a name in a field, an address on a return, and a knock that gets an answer, and none of those three things is a person.*

**She is not coming to the hearing and was not going to and was asked anyway, in effect, and came to the meeting on 24 February because a letter went past her office on the way to a museum and she read the envelope, and that is the whole of her reason and she has declined to give a better one.**

## S. Tully, about forty-five — assessor, Quiet Accord Registry — NO LONGER A NAME ON A ROTA

**Nineteen years in post. `QA/CR/1426`. She wrote *I am not sorry* on an acknowledgment on 30 December 2024 and she has never been spoken to by anybody in this case until Saturday the sixth of February 2025, and she said, in a corridor, that nobody had ever read that sentence back to her with the punctuation before.**

**In one morning she read her own refusal back at a room, produced two answers, issued one of them out of time and said so, told a registrar that there is no object in the Museum's file because a description is not a classification, refused to use the Registry's one place-category for a door mat, listed a question that has never been a determination, filled in box nine, found a man's name in a bureau field in four seconds, and said one sentence about legal neighbours and would not say it again.** She was right about every part of it and she is the reason the volume's question is a question and not a proposal. **She is not a friend and she is not a villain and she is not frightened and she did not have to do any of it.**

## A partner of Halliward Griswold & Reeve, about fifty-two — NEW IN THIS BATCH, UNNAMED

**Solicitor, 22 Pike's Buildings, Irving Quay, acting for Newton Bay (Estates) Limited. He is the first person outside this building who has asked Elias Venn a question, and the first person outside it who is not frightened of anybody in it, and he is not frightened because he has read their documents and thinks a great deal of them.**

**What he did:** read the Museum's minute of 27 January four times; came four days earlier than he asked to because the next cycle date is 1 April; told the Director, as a courtesy and not a threat, that her public meeting of 17 February is how a commercial programme left a museum; said his client's position is that it has no position; **asked the Registrar to say *a knock* again in the same words in March in front of nine people, and said that if she says it differently he will notice and will not mention that he has noticed, and declined to apologise for it, and said that was a description of how a hearing works**; and then asked the man in the coat whether he writes it down, and said three things: *I could probably get it, not lawfully, not easily, and I am telling you that I know that version exists* · *I am not going to do it, and I am not going to try, and I am telling you that I am not going to do it, and I want you to hear the shape of that sentence* · **if your evidence cannot be produced then it cannot be tested, and an untestable witness in a listed hearing is not a protection, it is a hole in the case with a person standing in it.** He is not a villain and he is not an ally and **he has done the most honest thing anybody has done to Elias Venn in twelve weeks and it was not kindness.**

## Mairin Devlin, 31 — registration officer

**Nine years, about eleven pounds an hour, a satchel and about eleven forms, and a pad of nine houses on Broad Street she has always had and has never had a list of.** In this batch she **refused to say the moral floor a second time before he had asked for it**, and when he used it at a door she said nothing, because it was being used and not asked for. She took him round five of the nine: two no answers, a man of about seventy who has never been on a roll and never wanted to be, a woman of about sixty who wanted to know once whether the roll costs anything, and then **number four, which she had never stood in front of, because the justification box on a special-visit form has wanted two lines for nine years and she has only ever been able to write *concern for vulnerable resident*, which is a lie everybody in that office knows is a lie — and on Thursday the eighteenth of February she wrote a true one: *address not in roll following recorded change of occupancy, possible omission, date of change not in local copy* — three things about documents, no word about a person, and it will be approved because it is not a lie.** She filled in a form on a doorstep in about a quarter of an hour, in a woman's own hand, for a household at number four that moved in on Sunday the twenty-ninth of November, and **she said the date of her return out loud, on a pavement, so that the woman could be held to it and not her to it: Wednesday the twenty-fourth of March, in the afternoon, and if she is not there by four o'clock you can ring the office and ask for her by name, which is the only thing anybody in that building can actually do.** She is not a friend and she is not a mystic and she has still not been thanked.

## Frank Dunning, about sixty-five — 88 Sillick Lane

**Keeper of the practice. Sixty-five, and has spent his working life standing still at a gate, and says he has done the thing Elias does four thousand times at one. Not frightened, not reassured, owed nothing, and on no list of people owed anything, and his tenancy ends on the twenty-second of August and will not be renewed and he has known since November.** In this batch he:
- **said no to the Director in about four seconds and gave the reason before he was asked for it** — no to the sheet, no to a copy, no to a list, no to a register, no to a rota, no to anybody coming round with a clipboard — *and I have been waiting since December for somebody to ask me so they could tell me I'd said no.*
- **was told by a Director that his block enters programme on the first of April and that his tenancy ends on the twenty-second of August, and said he had known since November and that nobody had ever said it out loud to him and everybody he had told had said *oh* and gone to their dinners**, and then put it on the sheet, because a date about a house is the only kind of thing that has ever gone on it.
- **asked Elias Venn whether he had a word, was told no, and said that a street does not own it either, because a street is a council with a different name and he has seen what a council does with a room; and then conceded the line itself: the third rule is in a different hand, has been on the sheet since 8 January, and he has never asked who wrote it and is not going to. What is his is the table, the kettle and the running of it at three o'clock, and if he stops there is no practice, and nobody is going to take that off him and nobody is going to keep it for me.** ⚠ **The practice's word is a permission and it belongs to a street. It does not become his, and it is not his authorship. Recorded because Chapter 77's first draft had him claiming both, which contradicted Mara Iles in the same scene and Chapter 80 eight days later.**
- **put the three weeks and a day into a sentence without being asked, over a kettle, in about eleven seconds: a date, a boundary, and a document somebody has to keep.**
- **said it is not a rescue, that nobody in that hall has been rescued, that nine blanks on a wire are still nine blanks, that a woman of eighty-one is not going back on that street until the middle of April, that a postman's round changed on the ninth of February and he does not know it and never will — and then asked anybody who thought they had been rescued to give their name so he could talk to them about it, and would do it nicely, and would do it in there.**
- **said it is not a covenant, and gave the reason, and said the thing the batch is built on: the minute somebody is keeping it, it is not what it is now; it would be the same three rules and there would be a fourth one that nobody wrote, and the fourth one would be *somebody is keeping it*.**
- **asked who is going to keep it, was told by the man in the coat to answer, and said *don't* — *you'd have done the thing you do, gone quiet and made it a moment, and about nine people would have felt better and one of them would have written it down* — and told him not to say he is not allowed into the building either, because that is the easiest sentence anybody has said to him in twelve weeks and it is worth nothing.**
- **changed the date on the registrar's four lines from the twenty-first to the twenty-second of March, because on the twenty-second his tenancy has still got four weeks and two days to run and he is still the man with the table.**

## A board trustee, about seventy — NEW IN THIS BATCH, UNNAMED

**Nine years on the board, a different person from Mrs Vessey, says almost nothing before lunch.** **He objected first and he asked for the word *hook* to be in the minute so that it would not be tidy, and his objection was not the name — the name is correct and he read the support — his objection was that this Museum told the public twice — in a public room in November and in a minute on 27 January — that it would not put a person's name in a public document, and here is a man's name going into a Board paper.** He withdrew it, asked that it be minuted that he objected first, and asked that the Chair's non-view be recorded because she is a lawyer. **And then he said he has been going to look at a building for nine years and did not know it was possible that one of its schedules could contain a person.** He is the first person in this case to make the Museum's own position the subject of an objection, and he was right and it was uncomfortable.

## The people of Broad Street — no name on any card and one name in the prose

**A woman of about thirty-four, alone, at number four in a road called Broad Street, who moved in on Sunday 29 November 2024, works, pays her rent, her tax and her bills, has voted since she was twenty-one, has never had a letter that was not returned, is on no list and is owed nothing.** She is on a registration officer's pad of nine houses, she was found by walking a street and not by any record, and **she is the only household in this batch that is actually helped by anything anybody did.** ⚠ **SHE IS NOT NAMED. Not in Chapters 71 to 80, not on a card, and not in a file, and she is not to be named in Volume 02 without a recorded decision, because the batch's best beat is Chapter 76, in which he tells her to her face that he is not going to write her name down and then does not write it. A state file asserted on 2026-09-26 that she *is* named in the manuscript; she is not, and that assertion has been removed. The rule he gave her is the second of the three lines at the top of the first page of his own notebook.**

**And she and Elias Venn are the only two people in this city in exactly the same position and neither of them knows the other exists, and that is not a coincidence and it is not a mystery: they are both owed nothing by the same chain, one of them is owed a knock and the other is owed a hearing, and neither of them is going to get one from this case.**

## Hattie Bramm, 54

**Not present in this batch and not asked. Her panel is ninety days old on 25 February, her refusal of 27 January is quoted by a Director in a hall, and her hundred and forty names are in a drawer and are not going anywhere.** **She has still not been asked a question by Elias Venn and this is the eighth batch in which that is true.**

## Doreen Sarn, 78

**Not present, not asked, not coming. Seven refusals. The seventh came back by post in four words on 29 January and is on a board and not in a file, because a list of people who have refused something is a list of people.**

## Mrs Toft, about fifty — records officer, district addressing service, Marsh Lane

**Six years in post, nine entries in a record of issue, never asked for anything until January.** In this batch she **said two words on a telephone that undid nine weeks of the case — *ring the water* — after being asked the right question for the first time in six years, which was *read me the person who is responsible for the connection of services*.** She has still not been thanked.

## Relationships, end of Volume 02 Batch 0003

- **Elias / Mara:** a working arrangement in which **she has now read her own letter into a statutory file in a box nobody had ever used, in order to put a question in front of the one office with jurisdiction, and he has watched her do it and understood that it is the same sentence as the minute of 27 January and that neither of them has ever said thank you.** No romance. No confession. No rooms. **He is not her project and she is not his instrument and the last thing they did together was disagree about a man's name on a piece of paper and be right about different halves of it.**
- **Elias / Ivo:** **a friendship in which a man of twenty-five has now told him, in a room, with witnesses, that the one thing he has been proudest of is a hole in the case with a person standing in it, and a man of thirty-two went and stood by a window.** Neither has said thank you. It is not going to be said. It does not need to be.
- **Elias / Frank Dunning:** **a working arrangement in which a man of sixty-five has now told him not to answer a question, and told him not to say the sentence he has been using instead of an answer since October, and the second of those is the more useful and cost him more.** They are not friends. They are not a department. **Nobody has asked either of them a question they could not answer and both of them know what the other one is owed, which is nothing, which is the correct arrangement.**
- **Elias / S. Tully:** **one morning, one room, and the only person outside this building who has ever read his case properly and spoken to him about it.** He is in no position. She is under no obligation. She filled in a box nobody had used and wrote a late answer out of time and said a sentence about legal neighbours once and would not say it again. **She has never been thanked and he has no way of thanking her and that is the right outcome.**
- **Elias / the solicitor:** **he was asked a question, and answered it, and was given the answer that his evidence cannot be tested, and did not argue.** A stranger, in a room, in company, on notice. **The first professional in this case to treat him as a witness rather than as a man who collects things, and he has not worked out what to do with that and has written it down and not torn it out.**
- **Elias / Sonia Boulton:** **she refused him a rent roll in about four seconds, through a records clerk, in December, before she knew his name, and was right, and he has still never met her in a room she arranged.** She read the programme out loud in one, and he was in it, and he was not the reason for any of it, and he has told nobody that he wished he had been.
- **Elias / Mairin Devlin:** **she told him the moral floor in January and he said it back to a stranger in her words in February, and she was not in the room and did not know and was not meant to.** She gave him her date out loud on a pavement. He has not thanked her. She would not want him to.
- **Elias / the bell at 11:52:** untouched, unspoken, and not referred to in this batch.
