**Nine hundred and four claims were opened between 9 and 19 December 2024 and she will not convert that into a number of households or a number of people.** About forty of the December claims have come to a point where the box is the thing in the way and nine have been refused on it, and forty is her pencil number. **She has refused nine claims since 8 December, eight of them of the Roisin Mellor shape, and the ninth is a man called Petrie who claimed in his wife's name for a flat in Ferry Bank he has never been inside, and section D exists because of him and because of November 1998, and she says the rule works and says it better than anybody has said it in this case and says it while it is happening to a woman she has liked for four minutes.** She will not start trying to work round her own rule for her, because the moment she does that it is a favour, and a file with a favour in it is a different animal.

**The excess is seven hundred and fifty pounds, once, per household, per event, and she has never in nine years seen it go to a kitchen, and she said so in a room a claimant could hear, having said in about nine rooms since December that she had never said it in one.** She asked Blanche Mellor, eighty-two, whether she was in that flat on the night of the ninth, and **did not write the answer down, and that is on the record in one line of a handling file and is the only line in nine hundred files that says a thing an office did not do.** She has said out loud that she wants to be able to say in a room that she cannot do one single thing with what she was told, and that she has wanted to since December.

**She chairs a meeting she has no authority to chair, having told the district office in writing and been told by nobody that they have read the letter. She says the cost out loud before anything else, in about nine seconds, and says it is not a virtue — it is that on 8 June a woman of thirty-four who had lost the ground floor of her flat asked her a question about a box she had been sitting in front of for nine years and she did not have an answer. On 28 June she read section B out in a hall with the windows open and said: that is what the box does, and it does not say what the box is for. And then she said the sentence she has used as a wall four times, and said it was a wall and was not an answer, and asked the question anyway, as a question and not a complaint, and asked that it be minuted, knowing there is no minute. ⚠ THE COUNT IN THIS BEAT IS FOUR, AND IT IS ONE COUNT AND NOT TWO. The question had been put to her four times in the three weeks before Monday 28 June and she had refused it every time with a sentence, so the sentence has been a wall four times. The first repair of this batch wrote *four refusals, three walls* on a guess about which quantity was which, and the chapter's own dialogue does not support the three. What is new on 28 June is the room: she says it out loud in front of nine people for the first time, says that it is a wall and is not an answer, and asks the question anyway. Corrected 2026-09-26.**

## GWILYM PETHERICK, about 55 — river engineer. NEW IN THIS BATCH.

**Thirty-one years, a van, a folding rule, a staff gauge about four feet long that lives in the back of the van, and a printed table in a plastic wallet laminated in about 2016 with two columns of numbers on it. He is fifty-five or fifty-six depending on which of his two birthday forms you believe.** He is the only person in the case who can turn a height into a level at a reach, and he is the only person who is not going to help.

**The table.** `BRAESWICH REACH — REDUCTION TO DATUM`. Table A: heights above Newlyn. Table B: heights above gauge zero, Braeswick pier gauge. **Note at the foot: the relationship between the two at this reach is not constant; do not convert one to the other by adding or subtracting a fixed figure.** Note 4: the offset between a sea datum and a river gauge zero changes as the bed between them changes; over 1998 to 2016 it moved by about 140 mm; figures printed before 1998 should not be converted using figures printed after it. **So the two numbers do not agree, have never agreed, and the reason is a hundred and forty millimetres of riverbed that has moved, and neither number is anybody's fault and neither is going to be repaired.** He says which he trusts and why: **he trusts the gauge zero, because the knots were tied by a man reading a gauge and a knot has no opinion about the sea** — and the other one is the one that is right, because it has a definition and a chain of custody and a document that anybody else is going to act on has to be in Newlyn, **and the difference between the two is between a hundred and thirty and about two hundred and seventy millimetres depending on the year.**

**And he will not give the table away, and he has said the reason out loud, and the reason is the volume's method in a professional's mouth:** the second it is in a hand that is not his it becomes a standard, and in about four years somebody will print a number off it on a reach where the bed is different and will not know that. **So the only numbers in this country for the height of the water at Braeswick are a painted board, a government gauge, and a laminated sheet in his own wallet. He has said he would like to be asked again in about four weeks in a different mood. ⚠ A later batch must be careful: if somebody asks him again in about four weeks, in the wrong mood, he will give it away, and then about a hundred and forty millimetres will be printed as a constant in about four hundred offices.**

**He is the instrument by which Volume 02's live question is answered and he does not answer it.** He laid the reach rope out against the wall under the 1911 board for about an hour, read eleven numbers, showed that nine of them are marks and two are a whipping and a seizing he has known about since 1994, and then **showed that every one of the nine lands within a centimetre or two of a line painted on a board that has been outside since 1911 — which is the only test there is — and wrote two words on his own sheet and underlined them: *these are heights. They are not levels.*** A height is a fact about a rope. A level is a fact about the river. To get from one to the other you have to know what the doorstep is, in the system the river is measured in, and that is a datum, **and a height without a datum is not wrong, it is useless, it is a different thing.** He has not said the sentence *is the pattern the object or the place* and nobody in that room made him say it, and when pressed he said he had said it in his own words.

**He also said the one thing he should not have said**: he has watched a flood wall come down on a good survey, and a number with a place on it stops being a measurement and becomes a claim about land, **and he is not going to be the man who printed a height for a street that has not got one** — which is the argument the city wins on in Chapters 146 and 147, and he is on the right side of it by accident. And on the night of the flood he asked for a three-centimetre forecast error to be written down in a place where somebody finds it in about four years, **and said he did not care what was written around it.**

## THE OTHERS, ALL COMPETENT, NONE A VILLAIN, NONE A FOOL

- **Colin Bray, about 44** — salvage contractor, nineteen years, two crews, four vans, a weighbridge at a yard eleven miles off the ring road, a docket book per crew. **Pays everybody on Friday, has missed two this year, told them before he missed them, and says both misses were his own fault because he put two vans on a street in March he should not have.** **Owed fourteen thousand one hundred and eighty pounds since 8 January by a managing agent whose head office is a floor above a man who took a call in February and said the same thing.** Told a street that, unasked, in about nine seconds. **The money is on the weight column, the column is never checked, and the crew writes what the client says.** He can tell you how many lines are on the pages of nine hundred December dockets and cannot tell you how many of them are wrong, **and that is the only number in his trade he would give anybody and he has not got it.** Asked a man with no post to write it down and was told there is nowhere to write it where anybody can check, and said: then write it down in a place where you cannot, and I am aware of how that sounds.
- **Dermot Slade, about 34** — writes the dockets, in block capitals because it gets wet. **Writes what the client tells him and does not ask them to prove it, and gives the reason in one sentence: the alternative is a scale in a front room on a Saturday morning and a man out of work on Monday.** Refused a stranger a photograph of a page of his book in about four seconds.
- **Wendy Ockley, about 54** — one van, four people, twenty-six years in the salvage trade. **Saw the June stain and identified it in about nine seconds — water through a ventilation, settling into the top of a wall as it goes down — and said the tide would be above the June mark again inside a fortnight and that everybody in the city knew it.** **Defended a governance rule that has cost her and cost the case a photograph, and said the rule is not stupid, and said it stopped about four hundred exposures of which she had personally seen about nine and every one of them was a lie and she could not have proved one.** Told Ivo Pell that somebody in a building on a hill had cost him the clean one and meant it kindly.
- **Yvette Carrow, 48 or 49** — about eleven years in the post, Flood Emergency Powers, City Flood Office. **Nobody sent her; a preliminary view sheet said *building* and she walked four hundred feet along a quay and stopped at a wall.** Spent fifty minutes with a torch rather than nine, on the advice of a salvage contractor: *if you take nine you will write a preliminary view and if you take forty you will write a different one, and mine is free and yours is not.* Stood on a stone revetment with a torch for fifty minutes on Wednesday 23 June with a salvage contractor of twenty-six years and found about nine centimetres of horizontal movement in a joint, and the pair of them said nothing about it for about eleven seconds. ⚠ **The tape reading is not hers: a licensed officer measured that joint herself on the Thursday morning and got nine centimetres and would not argue with the nine centimetres. Two measurements, two days, two people, one figure, and the earlier state files had merged them.** **Signed `Schedule F` at about ten past seven on the Wednesday evening, having said the cost out loud twice first**, and the second half of the cost is the half that will get quoted. **And said the thing that is the antagonist of this movement, out loud, in a corridor, to a man who had not asked: I have got no power in this form to give anybody a reason. I have got no power to see anybody's claim, their tenancy, their letter, their photographs, or anything at all about any person affected. So I am not suspending access to a building. I am suspending access to a building without ever having to know who is in it, and I have discovered that in about ten minutes that this is the easiest instrument I have ever signed and I would like somebody to explain to me why that is a bad thing.** Nobody explained it to her. **She is proved right about the water in the middle of the volume and that is the point of her.**
- **Prudence Halloway, 74** — freeholder of the bonded store. **Has been inside it once, in 1987, when her predecessor showed it to a prospective tenant and died that August.** Says so at the head of a letter so that nothing after it can be read as a denial. **Never asked what was on the other side of her own wall in nineteen years of rent, and says she thought about that for twenty minutes and got it wrong about six hours too late.** Refuses the word outrageous, apologises for putting a city document on the wrong clipboard and corrects herself at once, and says she is not going to insult a very good week of being frightened. Asks the only question anybody asked on that quay: *two weeks. Somebody in an office has put two weeks in a box. What happens on the ninth of July if nothing has changed.*
- **Ama Osei, about 36** — a licensed officer, eleven bonded stores done. **Put her refusal in her own file at nine in the morning, before anybody argued with her:** she is not going to cut the shutter, a bond is a seal broken in front of her or not at all, and she cannot be on the quay and not in the building, and there is no version of this where the two things are compatible. Asked whether she is on the same side as them, said: **I am on the same side as the bank. I am not on anybody's side, and I would like it recorded that the two of those sentences are different and that I have thought about the difference for about a minute and a half and I am still not sure which one is true.**
- **Corry Slatter**, flood night crew supervisor, six years on the rota, a crew of four and a crew of three. Says at ten past nine on the Friday: **we are not a rescue service, we are a council night crew with a pump and a boat and four names on a sheet.** Writes the board. **Says the Monday before it happens so that somebody writes it down, and when a man with no post says he is not a person who minutes anything, asks him not to let good news be a thing two men know about.**
- **Ferris**, a pump operator at South Sluice, nineteen years on that bank, thirty-four. **Is the man who told a stranger where a city record is, on the second day after it went under four feet of water, and said he would like it noticed that he thought about it for two days first.** Also the man whose pump and generator came to a wharf on the Monday.
- **Jim Tarrant, 58** — twenty-two years a removals man. **Went into the store at about eleven o'clock on the Friday with a torch, a key for the office two doors along and no key for the store, whose side door was off its latch, and did not tell anybody, and was in there four hours, and was got out at about twenty past three over a yard in a boat with a ladder, and was not hurt.** Moved about nine boxes to the top tier and did not open one. **He is the only living person who can say what was on the bottom tier of that racking, and what he can say is: tons of paper, in files, and he did not look at any of them — and then the true version, which is worse, that a guess would have been better than what he has given us because a guess would have gone in a book.**
- **Roisin Mellor, 34** — ground floor at 2 Cutler's Rise, Ferry Bank, with her grandmother. **Claim `DL/24/10455` refused on 8 June for want of a date of loss, item 4 left empty, no reason recorded because none was given and none was asked for.** Needs seven hundred and fifty pounds for a boiler and about five weeks of arrears. **Obtained her own eleven-sheet claim file on Tuesday 22 June and it came on Friday the twenty-fifth, and she asked that it be said in a room that she was on hold for eleven minutes and then told the truth by a woman who did not have to tell her anything, and that neither of those things is remarkable and both of them are why she is sitting in the hall.**
- **Blanche Mellor, 82** — was in that flat on the ninth of December and on the tenth and was got out at about two in the morning by two men from the floor above with a torch and a rope; in a first-floor flat in her sister's since the fifteenth. **Came to the office because she was asked whether she wanted to come, had her coat on, and said two things: forty years of washing and a cooker and my mother's chair, and it is nine words and one of them is *unless*. Asked whether she was in the flat on the night of the ninth and said yes, and on the tenth, and that she was got out of it at about two in the morning — and it was not written down.**
- **Kathleen Orrey, 61** — over a shop in Fisher Lane, nineteen years running a stationery and newsagent's. **Claim `DL/24/10207` since 12 December 2024, unsettled, item 4 left empty rather than write something untrue.** Got a second letter on 3 June about a flood in June and has not had June — she has had December three times. **Says, unprompted, that she has never been difficult about a piece of paper in her life, somebody has to ask and I answer.** ⚠ **And at the end of the Friday the fourth of June she said the thing nobody else in this case has said about the two pages: they came with the *first* letter, in December, and have been on the shelf under her counter ever since with a rubber band on them, and she has not opened them because she has a shop to open. She handed them to a man with no post and asked for them to be taken back to the office they came from, because they are hers and not theirs, and she would like them back when this is over. He said he would bring them back himself. She said he would not, and that it was not a criticism, and that nobody comes back. Recorded in the review-fix pass of 2026-09-26; the object and the promise are carried at `state/open-threads.md` after Volume 03 Batch 0001, item 11.**
- **The woman of about forty-three at 14 Ashgill Row, Bankfoot. NOT NAMED, BY DESIGN.** She is at the door, the water came in to fourteen inches in the front room, the carpet from about 1994 is gone and the boards under it are black and a man is coming on Wednesday about the joists, the boiler from about 1978 is not wet and will last the winter if she believes a man she cannot pay, her mother is at number six and eighty-nine with a bed in the front room of her mother's front room. **She told Elias, on a kerb on Ferry Bank on the Monday evening, in about nine seconds, that there is a building in Bankfoot where the water went in and there is nobody anybody can ring; he asked her how she knew and did not ask her the second question, and the second question was the one he wanted.** She was in that building on the night of the eighteenth and she went in to get somebody out and the person was not there. **She is not a device, she is not asked anything about the case, she is not asked her name by anybody, and a council contractor asked and she gave him a number and a floor and nothing else. ⚠ WHAT SHE IS FOR IS CHAPTER 129. Nothing in this batch prepares it and nothing in this batch should be read as preparing it.**
- **The woman of about sixty-one on a quay on 15 June** — a photograph of the December water on her front-room floor with the ninth of December in the corner of the frame, a dado rail, a radiator, her mother's chair upside down on the table. **Asked whether it will get her anything and told the truth by a river engineer: I do not know. I am a river engineer and I have no idea what a photograph does to a claim and I would not guess in front of you. That is the first honest thing anybody in this business has said to me since December.**
- **Emrys Tasker, about 61** — Braeswick Lower Lock since 1988, and the one before that. **Fifty years of a habit with no ceremony in it: gets a ladder out, puts the board back up, looks at where the water got to, and ties a knot in the line so that he does not have to remember the number.** Says the board is a ruler and not a gauge and that if you lose the pad the year is gone. Says the knots are a mark and that there is nothing else and he has never told anybody anything else. Asked which year on the canvas tag means what, says 1968 is either the year the rope was made or the year the board was hung and he asked the water authority eleven years ago and was told it did not matter and **was told the man was not entirely right.**
- **Mara Iles, 28** — see the relationships above. **Also: she has been in this case for five years and this is the first time she has read a professional instrument and found a hole in it that is not a matter of taste, and she wants that on the record somewhere that is not a public document.**
- **Anja Ward, 53** — see the relationships above. **Also: has been in post nineteen years and says there is a great deal of that nineteen years which consists of being careful about the wrong things.**
- **Ivo Pell, 25** — see the relationships above. **Also: the condition of 1 December 2024 has never been tested at its edge and he has now asked for it to be tested twice in six months, by a Director and by a stranger, and got one answer on the fourteenth of June and no answer on the fifth, and wrote UNDERNEATH his own exposure COUNTERSIGNATURE UNAVAILABLE — ONE MEMBER OF STAFF PRESENT, and said it would be a problem for somebody in about four years.**

## THE RESIDENTS OF SILLICK LANE — WHAT MOVEMENT 1 ADDED, AND THE BIBLE DECISION IS UNCHANGED

**They are still a group and not a cast, and the reason is the manuscript's own argument: a list of houses is worse than a list of people, and a bible cast is a list of people.** Nothing in this batch promotes anybody. What Movement 1 added is four things about them, and they are on the page:

- **The rents are a hundred and twenty pounds a week between seven households in four flats, and two of the four flats were empty at four o'clock on the first day of the term with the rent already paid that morning, and the housing officer said she chose one date on purpose instead of four and said why.**
- **Clause 7 of page two is the reason the owner of freehold has never been up those stairs and is not going to start, and she read it out to an empty corridor, and nobody argued with it.** The landlord is not a party to any practice, arrangement, custom or agreement between the residents, and the residents are not parties to anything the owner does in the building. **That clause is a wall that protects a landlord from a street, and it is also the reason nobody in Cauldwell Buildings can be asked what anybody does on the first floor, and nobody in this batch noticed that it cuts both ways except the woman of about fifty who said it cuts both ways and then said nothing else about it.**
- **Clause 14 of page three has not been used and will not be used before October, and nobody asked who would use it.**
- **Vera Pilling said out loud, in a hall, on 28 June 2025, that she chose the first of October for the same reason a man of fifty-two did, and that neither of them has ever said in a room what happens on that day, and that she is going to keep not saying it.** ⚠ **And the man of thirty-two is the only person in the city who has noticed that neither of them has.**

**And Mavis Ludlow, 61, of 22 Sillick Lane, was told the truth in a corridor on Sunday 4 July 2025 by a registrar who had a duty, and the truth is that this flood produces one kind of paper and it has a date on it and the only date it wants is a date of loss, and a loss is a thing that has happened to a thing, and she has not lost a house, and so there is no version of this in which she is written down at twenty-two Sillick Lane in June 2025.** She worked out the rest herself in about nine seconds, was not asked, and says nobody has lied to her — **and then said the thing about the register that is the loudest open thread in the manuscript, unasked, because that is why she said it.**

---

# CHARACTER STATE — VOLUME 03, BATCH 0002 (Chapters 111–120), as of Sunday 8 August 2025

**Verified against `chapters/volume-03/chapter-0111.md` through `-0120.md` and not against the rest of this state layer.** Movement 2 is complete as drafted. **Nobody in this batch is promoted to `bible/characters.md` on usefulness.** One person earned an entry on her own evidence and is recorded here first: **Averil Shand**, whose eleven refusals and one facsimile are the batch's argument given to a woman with a form in her hand. **Hesta Rell was already in `bible/characters.md` and is now on the page for the first time in three volumes, four volumes late, and the displacement is recorded in `outline/volume-03.md` as a hard lock and not hidden.**

## THE RELATIONSHIPS, ONE LINE EACH, AND NONE OF THEM IS SETTLED

- **Elias / Mara — paying for it, not declaring it, and the paying got expensive.** She is on the page in five of the ten chapters and not in five, and that is a person with a job. **She went into a ward this case had been in once, in the rain, in June, and asked a question nobody has ever asked a person *and written down*, and got four words, and wrote them down, and read them back, and a woman of about forty-three corrected one word and was right.** She invented a form on a doorstep, gave it a reference in a private series, left a name line empty on purpose with the form saying so, and kept it out of the collections system and out of every claim file, and said the reason: a person who has not asked to be held is not a collection. **She read a shrine's book and said it is better than anything the Museum produces and that she cannot put it in a card, and she put her hand on the fact that there is no line on a card for a room, which she has known since 2019.** ⚠ **She telephoned a registrar of sixty-eight at eleven at night and got the second minute in this case, and she is the reason it exists.** The first was R. Iqbal's on 31 July, and Chapter 120 said otherwise and was corrected in the review-fix pass of 2026-09-26. **On the Saturday she also read a six-sheet claim file out loud to Elias Venn in a corridor on Wharf Street, found a line on it that says an office told the truth on 8 June, and established that the claimant cannot be given it, because one page of a file is the whole file. That corridor is the batch's clearest statement of what she is and what she has not been given, and she has no instrument for it.** And she wrote a man's name and a date on a card because the only instrument anybody in this case has is a name against a date, and if she does not use it on him she is only saving it for somebody worse. **Neither of them uses the word, and the word is not printed anywhere in the ten chapters, and the page says so twice.**
- **Elias / the professionals — he is in the room and he is the one who is not a party, and it is not a compliment.** A housing officer of fifty-eight said on Friday the thirty-first of July that he was useful and in the same breath that there is no box on earth on which he is a party. **He said no to a fourth thing in five weeks, in advance, in a room, before anybody asked, and she wrote *not asked. says no first* on a pad and turned it round so eleven people could see it.** On Tuesday the fourth of August he was in a room where a sheet of A4 was produced and he was the only person who did not take a copy, and he noticed it, and he wrote that down and nothing else. **On Friday the sixth of August he was asked nothing, and it was the fifth attempt in five weeks, and he says in the room that if he now says who may speak for a community then in four years there is a man in a room who can say the man who said it had no post, and he would be right.**
- **Elias / Hesta Rell — the first person in three volumes who asks him a question.** He has no answer to it and did not pretend to, and he gave the reason, and she said it was the first honest thing anybody had said to her about it since March. **He is not the person who refused her list of four and must not be.** She has a second question, worse, about a room with about eleven people in it, and she has said out loud that she is not asking it and wants about three weeks of watching him not know the answer to the first. **He notices that she did not say the word, and she notices that he did not ask, and both of those are the same competence and neither of them is trust.**
- **Elias / Colin Bray — the only professional in this case who has asked him for something twice and got a different answer both times.** On the seventh of July the man of forty-four sent forty-nine pages of his own book to a claims handler because she asked in a form, and said the reason he had refused a stranger in June and refused her in July is the same: a stranger would use it. **On the thirty-first of July he said out loud in a room that a line of his is evidence of an event and a line of his is evidence of a person who was frightened, and that he has been carrying both in the same column since December and nobody has ever asked him about the second one.** He is owed money for December across about forty addresses and has not been paid since the eighth of January, and a claims handler with a duty could do nothing about it and he did not appear to expect her to.
- **Elias / the woman of about forty-three — he was on her step twice and did not go in either time, and the second time he did not go because going would have made him the second knock.** In June he said three things from a step and a fourth after them, and the fourth was that he would not ask whether there was anybody in the building. In July a registrar asked and got four words. **He was not the one who asked and he is not the one who wrote it down, and the only trace of that door in the world is a sheet of A4 with an empty name line, and it is not his.**
- **Elias / Ivo Pell and Anja Ward — neither appears in the prose of these ten chapters.** Ivo's condition of 1 December 2024 is referred to once, through Fenn's sentence of 25 February 2025, and Anja's letter of the twenty-eighth of May is referred to once, as a public document that names no person, inside a list of what is to be read. **Neither is a gap that needs filling and neither is a silence anybody should read as anything.**
- **Elias / Hattie Bramm — still not asked, and the silence is now thirty-five weeks and it is deliberate on her side and uncomfortable on his.**
- **Elias / Cliff Sowerby — still not rung.** The fortnight he asked for ran out in June. **It is still the only outstanding thing he is aware of that he has chosen to leave, and he wrote it down once and it has not been on the page since.**

## MARIANNE COPE, about 34 — flood claims handler, City Flood Office, Wharf Street. Answered the question, and cannot do one single thing about it.

**She said it out loud on Monday the fifth of July 2025, in a room with four people in it, in about forty seconds, in her own words, for the first time in nine years: a date of loss is for attaching an excess to a household, and the excess is a flat sum, once, per household, per event, and the household is the policyholder at an address on the date, and where the date is not evidenced the settlement cannot be attached.** She said it as a schedule, a figure and an attachment, and she did not use the words *excess settlement* once. **She has known since about March 2016, has said the purpose out loud to about four people, and two of them have left the industry. It is not a secret. It is page two, section B, in four lines, issued to four hundred and eleven people in this city, and there has never once been a meeting about it.**

**And then she said what it does, and the half that matters is not the machinery: a household cannot produce a date when the only documents that would evidence it are inside the building, and the building is the thing that went.** A rent letter, a meter, a bank, a doctor, a tenancy — all in the flat, all under eleven inches of water under a staircase, and a wardrobe from about 2011 on its side in two feet of silt.

**She can do one single thing about none of it, and she lists six, and every one is true**, and the sixth is that there is no fund for what the decision has actually cost a household and she has looked twice. **She was not rescued in this batch and is not rescued later in the volume, and the reason she is trapped is the reason she is the one who knows.**

**The weight column is hers too, and it is the same woman, and that is the batch's central object.** She obtained forty-nine dockets lawfully, because the only way to get a page of a contractor's book is to pay a claim the page supports. Nine of them are a number that is not what a lorry carried. **She will not give the number of nine hundred and something, and the reason is that a hundred and eighty is a number that would be on a wall by Tuesday, and she has forty-nine dockets and about nine hundred and six December attendances.** She keeps the nine addresses in a pad in a locked drawer and has not looked one of them up, and the reason is not a rule: **if she finds out about them they stop being a line in a column and start being nine women, and then the arithmetic becomes a decision, and she is not the person who decides.** Four reasons none of the nine can be prosecuted, all correct, and the fourth is that pursuing it means pursuing nine households, every one of which is owed the same seven hundred and fifty pounds and none of which has ever had it.

**She refuses twice to convert a number of people into a proportion, and she gives the reason the second time, and it has not changed since June.** She chairs a room she has no authority to chair and says the cost out loud first. **She will not write to the owner of a general claims manual and says why, and the reason is correct and costs something.** She has a pencil note in the back of a plastic wallet with about forty claims in it and it goes in a bin when she leaves. **And on Friday the fourth of June she wrote a line in a handling file that is the only line in about nine hundred files that says an office told somebody the truth — *applicant advised re item 4; no reason recorded* — and it is on the office's file and not the claimant's, and the claimant does not know it exists, and a later batch must not let it surface in an email or a meeting.**

## GWILYM PETHERICK, about 55 — river engineer. Asked again, in the wrong mood, and did not say which way he went.

**He has the only conversion in the county in a buttoned pocket and he is the only person who is not going to help, and on Friday the twenty-third of July 2025 he was asked again and the asking was need rather than rudeness, and he said so before anybody accused him of anything.** A statutory officer with a bank to say something about and a form with a box in it, and the box gets filled in on Monday whether or not he says a word.

**He gave four reasons in the order he has given them three times since March, and he said the order is the argument**, and then **a fifth reason he has not used, and he said it was the only true one of the five: the sheet is not a document, it is a job, and he updates it about twice a year out of nine years of his own readings, and the second it is a document it stops being a job and becomes a thing that was true in 2016.** He said the other four are about what other people would do with it, and that he has let the man of thirty-two believe otherwise three times and should not have, because the fourth of the four is about a flood wall and it is not his to be afraid of.

**He gave one of six reasons and kept five, and the one he gave is that he does not want his name on a thing used at South Sluice to decide whether a pump runs, and a man who ties a knot in a line every spring is two hundred yards from that pump house and he has known him thirty-seven years.** He set a condition and it is not about the form: *tell me what happens to a street if the number is wrong.* He said out loud that he has made the question harder on purpose so that he has time, and a woman of forty-eight said that is a trick, and he said it is a man who wanted to be asked again in about four weeks. **The wallet went on the bonnet of a van, latch side up, unopened, and back into a buttoned pocket. He did not say which way he went and the chapter does not decide it.**

**He converted no height into a level in this batch and answered no question about the pattern.** The object-or-place question was available for the sixth time, in a car park, and was not asked, and he said: *you have nearly asked me that question about four times now, in three different rooms, in three months, and you have not asked it once, and I have watched you not ask it four times.* **The answer remains a number beside a knot in Chapter 146.**

## AVERIL SHAND, about 68 — registrar of burials for the parish of Braeswick, forty-one years. NEW IN THIS BATCH, and she earns an entry on her own evidence.

⚠ **A parish registrar of burials is not a Museum registrar and the two must never be put in one sentence.** Mara Iles says so out loud inside four minutes of arriving at the vestry, and says that four of the nine objects she keeps records of are about people and none of those four are the people themselves.

**She keeps a hardback of interments about four inches thick on a shelf in a vestry above a radiator with a dehumidifier on the floor.** About a hundred and forty years of a river valley's record of who died in it, in a room with a door on it, and **nobody in that parish knows what it is except her, and she has not told them in forty-one years.**

**A page is not a list, and the argument is this manuscript's own in another mouth: a page is a record of what happened on one day at one churchyard, it has a date at the top, the names go down it in the order they came in, and the order has a shape a list has not got; a list is a set of names anybody can read in order, anybody is the whole of the problem, and a list cannot be accounted for.** She has been asked for a list forty-one times' worth and has given eleven. **The first went out in 1986 to a woman forty miles away looking for a man drowned in 1949; the list was in date order; the woman worked from the front and did not get to it and told the family he was not there; Averil Shand found the entry on the fourteenth of June 1990, on the page for the eleventh of March 1949, and the family have had a different story since and it is theirs.** Two of the eleven are in her minute book with the dates in and she has not got a column for what she lost.

**She will not let anybody photograph the book, including a man from the water authority who stood in front of it for nine minutes in 2014 with a telephone in his hand. She photocopies one page, tears the stub off, and accounts for it.** The page is the ninth of December 2024 and the fourth interment on it is `E. SOWERBY, 91, of The Paddock, Nether Braeswick` and the column headed for a stone says **no memorial to be provided, family cannot**, and a registrar of twenty-eight read that line and said it is the first line she has ever read that tells her about a family in this city that could not pay for a stone, and then said she can do nothing with it in four words, and the four words are the reason this batch exists.

**She is not a keeper of secrets and she is not a villain, and she does not know what is in a bonded store on a wharf four miles downriver, and nobody told her, and the person who decided not to tell her was Mara Iles, and the reason took about eleven seconds: a woman who has been asked for a list eleven times is the last person in this city who should be told there is another one.** Nobody improved on it.

**And she is the person who minuted the question nobody answered**, by telephone, at about eleven at night on Friday the sixth of August, in a minute book belonging to a parish, and she was not in the room, and she says so in the fourth item, and asks for that to be read out to whoever reads it and to the woman who asked it. **She has kept a minute book for forty-one years and has never minuted a meeting she was not at, and she gave the reason, and the reason is that a registrar of burials is asked for a list eleven times in forty-one years and she was not going to be asked for the whole of a meeting and get nothing, which is what happens every time a thing is not written down.**

## HESTA RELL, 61 — keeper of the Last Cup. ON THE PAGE FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THREE VOLUMES.

⚠ **`outline/series.md` opens the Elias–Hesta theological argument in Volume 02. `grep -riE "hesta|last cup"` over Volume 01 and Volume 02 returned zero. Volume 02 is closed and may not be rewritten, and `outline/series.md` and `outline/ending.md` are not edited in this phase, so the honest position is recorded: the line opens in Volume 03 and runs V3–V6 in practice, four volumes instead of five, and Volume 06's beat — a god offering a useful practice in exchange for a promise a town cannot afford — is unaffected by losing Volume 02.** The displacement is a hard lock in `outline/volume-03.md` and is not hidden.

**She is the first person in three volumes of this case who is not a municipal employee, and the first who asks Elias Venn a question instead of answering one.** She is not a mystic and there is no god in the room. **The Last Cup is a room with a door and a table and a list of four names taped to the door frame, nineteen years old, and before that three.** What happens in it: somebody who is dying and has nobody is brought to a door and two of the four go and sit with them, for as long as it takes, and one washes them and one tells the house, and **if there is no house then one of them walks about four miles to a register and gives the information.** That walk is the whole of what this case needed and nobody knew it: it is how a man of ninety-one got onto a page, and she telephoned the parish registrar herself on the eighth of December 2024 because there was nobody inside four miles, and a registrar of twenty-eight wrote down four conditions and one of them was that if the page goes anywhere she is to be asked first, and that is why she came down a hill.

**She will not give the list of the four, and the reason is this case's argument said by somebody who has never read this case: a list of names at an address on a date is a household; a household is a thing that can be attached to a schedule; a schedule pays a flat sum to a household; and four people who open a door on a Tuesday have no form, no reference, no office and no way of being summoned, so there is nothing to refuse you with.** She has had about nine people want that list since 1994 and eleven more since March, and she had never said the reason to anybody who was going to write it down before Elias Venn. **A door on a Tuesday, a table, and four people who are not employees of anything, is the whole of the protection, and she says it is not more than that, and she names two women who are not in the room.**

**Her question, six words, at about ten past four on Monday the twenty-sixth of July 2025: is a place allowed to say no.** He has no answer and does not pretend to, and the reason is that he has been refused four times by people and has a shape for that, and nobody has ever refused him a place, and he has written twice in his own book that a building cannot answer, and both of those were convenient. She says it is the first honest thing anybody has said to her about it since March. **Her position: she wants a god remembered in a place and he wants a god remembered accurately, and the argument is about who may speak for a community and about whether a hidden practice that saved people was right, and neither of them is right, and this volume does not settle it.**

**The shrine's own book: dates down the left, what was done in the middle, and nothing on the right. There are no names in it and it was not designed that way as a protest.** A name once went in, and after that the four could not have a conversation about a Tuesday without somebody being frightened; a house once went in, and a house with a name on it and a Tuesday in it is the exact shape of the thing ruining this flood. **A registrar of twenty-eight read it and said it is better than anything the Museum produces, and that she can fill in five of the six lines of a card, and the maker is four people who have not agreed to be the maker of a record, and there is no line on a card for a room, and she has known that since 2019 and has never had to put her hand on it.** She then asked one question too many about a person, was told the truth correctly, and did not like it, and that is the reading that did not go well.

**Three promises she has made out loud and a later batch must not lose.** That she has a second question, worse, about a room with about eleven people in it who do not agree about anything, and that she is not asking it, and that she wants about three weeks of watching a man not know the answer to the first. That the book may be asked for in about a fortnight and that she has not decided. That the registrar should be asked again **in September and not before**, and that the reason is that she came on a Monday morning to a room above a shop and asked one question and read a book for two hours and did not once ask whether she had a religion, and that nobody from that building has done that in five years. ⚠ **And she is the one who said *the one who is there when it is inconvenient* when the movement's question was put, and then said, before anybody decided it was an answer, why it is not one, and that she has been asked this question three times in her life and has not answered it on any of the three. The volume does not settle it and neither of them settles it.**

## NEIL BRAITHWAITE, 58 — technical liaison, policy instructions, Claims Instructions Library, Wharf Street. NEW IN THIS BATCH, and he is not a villain.

**Nineteen years in post. Alive. In post. He adopted section B into a two-page instruction on the sixth of March 2011, verbatim, from Part 9 of a general claims manual in force from 2009 and not reissued, and he did not write it and has never written it.** He read Part 5 as a whole once, in 2011, and has read section B since about six times. **He says the correct place to raise whether the words are wrong is the manual's owner, and that it is not him and it is not them, and he says that as a fact about a building and not as a way of getting rid of them.** Asked whether he agrees with the section, he says **no**, and says he had nineteen seconds to find a better sentence and had not got one, and that he noticed.

**He has been asked in writing whether he thinks the 2011 instruction is fit for the purpose it is used for, and he has not yet answered, and this batch does not resolve it. Volume 04 must not make him a villain and must not require him to answer, because there is no instrument in this case that can do either.** He is the first person this manuscript's only instrument has caught who has not asked to be caught, and the reason he was caught is that he answered a question in his own job truthfully, in his own hand, in his own form, on a Friday, and there was no box on the form that would have let him answer without his name in it. **A registrar of twenty-eight wrote his name and the word *alive* and the words *in post* and *no wrongdoing identified* on a card, and said the reason is that the only instrument anybody in this case has ever had is a name against a date, and if she does not use it on him she is only saving it for somebody worse, and she does not want to be a person who keeps it.**

## THE OTHERS, ALL COMPETENT, NONE A VILLAIN, NONE A FOOL

- **Sinead Molloy, 47** — same claims handling team as Marianne Cope, twenty-two years, never handled that claim, present at a review because a review is attended by somebody other than the person who made the decision. **She has read page one, in about 2016, called section B elegant, and has never been asked a second question about it, and that is on the page and is a hole in a profession.** In Chapter 120 she is the person who would have to type the publication and says out loud that she is there for that reason, and she refuses to type *the City Flood Office has consulted with the residents* because it is a false statement, and says she will resign.
- **Roisin Mellor, 34** — her claim reached the thirtieth day and closed on Monday the fifth of July. **She asked for a review in writing because her grandmother of eighty-two does not know what day it is and she wanted somebody to say the date out loud to her in a room.** She worked out the whole of the second floor herself in about nine seconds in a hall in June and is not asked anything about it, and she said so herself, and she was not going to be asked it again.
- **The woman of about forty-three at 14 Ashgill Row, Bankfoot. STILL NOT NAMED, BY DESIGN.** She is at the door, she is not asked anything about the case, and **in Chapter 113 she is asked one question, about a building on a night, and answers it in four words, and the four words are written down and read back to her and she corrects one of them.** Nobody asks her name and the name line on the form is left empty on purpose. **What she is for is Chapter 129, and nothing in this batch prepares it and nothing in this batch may be read as preparing it.** She has rung the claims office once, on the twenty-fourth of June, and was told the truth and could not be helped, and has been extremely angry about it for three weeks and has now got off the telephone.
- **The woman of seventy-nine at a house in Ferry Bank. STILL NOT NAMED.** Nineteen years in the house, seventy-nine next year, came out onto her step in a cardigan at about ten past eight on Saturday the fifth of June and said no to a crew in her own house in about forty seconds, and was right. **The only record of her anywhere is one line in nine hundred and six salvage dockets that says a crew did not attend and removed nothing**, and a contractor of forty-four has twice called it the best line in his book and worth nothing, and it turns out that it is good for exactly one thing. **She is now on a sheet in a drawer in a temporary office on Ferry Bank because she said no, and that is the whole of her appearance in this batch, and a man of thirty-two wanted to go to her door and did not go.**
- **Rukhsana Iqbal, 47** — resilience coordinator for the district, six years, convened the meeting in Chapter 118 because it is her job and because three offices had each told her in writing they were producing something. **She says the cost out loud first, and says that if the decision is to do nothing she will be the person who decided to do nothing, in writing, with her name on it, and that she has never put her name on a *nothing* before.** She put four numbers on a whiteboard in pencil with a line across the top saying that if anybody joins them up in a document she will resign, and she signed the first document in this case that somebody has put their own name under. She came back into the Chapter 120 meeting for the last hour, having not been there for the first, and she chaired a question.
- **Neil Braithwaite** — see above.
- **Yvette Carrow, 48 or 49** — eleven years in post. **She came on the Friday night of Chapter 120 with a printed sheet face down on the table, and said the reason out loud before asking the question, and said the question is not going to be one she answers herself.** She is proved right about the bank and about the tide again. **She has a statutory power over a quay and not one square inch of power over a person, and she says so, and she put her name against a gap in a list of ten, which is the first time anybody in this case has put their name against a gap.** Her second instrument is not decided and she has said in advance that she will decide in an office on a Tuesday and not on a quay.
- **Ama Osei, 36** — licensed officer, eleven of these. **She was on the quay at a quarter past nine on the morning the suspension expired, with the notice countersigned, and she said the sentence that is the whole of that quay: nobody ever designed a piece of paper for the morning after, and a morning after is not a period, and it does not have a box in it.** She is still not able to enter an office along the quay while the ground is live, and she is not going to pretend it is not because the form has stopped.
- **Colin Bray, 44** — see the relationships above. **Owed money since the eighth of January across about forty addresses; sent forty-nine pages of his own book to a claims handler in a form; said in a room that a line of his is evidence of an event and of a person who was frightened.** Not a cartel, not the face of one, and he is owed money and says so.
- **Emrys Tasker, 61** — Braeswick Lower Lock since 1988. **He is two hundred yards from a registrar of burials he has known for forty years and has never asked her the question, and in Chapter 114 he concedes the page argument to her and not to the other two, and says he wants a knot in a line, which is the same thing, and is the reason he has got one and she has got a book.** The rope is not mentioned in the prose of this batch and the third ward-bell does not appear.
- **V. Pilling, 81, of 6 Sillick Lane** — keeper of the register. **Not in the room in Chapter 120 and not asked to be, and named as item six of a list of six things the case has not done, with about nine gaps and about four hundred more to come, a drawer that does not lock on purpose, a date on the inside front cover, and no names in the book.** She has said in front of nine people that she is not going to be asked, and she was not asked, and a woman of forty-eight with a statutory power said in a room that she is the fourth person in the case to notice that neither of them has ever said what happens on the first of October and the first with a power to do anything about it, and she is leaving it.
- **Mara Iles, 28; Anja Ward, 53; Ivo Pell, 25; Hazel Cray, 58; Frank Dunning, about 65; Mavis Ludlow, 61** — see the relationships and the thread list. ⚠ **Hazel Cray is the person in this batch who is right about a list and wrong about a person, and she knew she was going to be from Tuesday night and came anyway, and said so in a room, and it is the most honest thing anybody in this batch did.** ⚠ **She is also the person who said out loud, in the room and about nine minutes after she had signed, that the minute she had just written contradicts itself — item one forbids a list of addresses and item three builds one out of about nine hundred — and that she had the docket round on a screen before she wrote either item, and that she was saying it in a room rather than going home to initial a sixth one, because a contradiction only one person knows about is not a record. Added in the review-fix pass of 2026-09-26 and it is the most useful thing she does in the batch.**

---

# CHARACTER STATE — VOLUME 03, BATCH 0003 (Chapters 121–130), as of Tuesday 7 September 2025

**Verified against `chapters/volume-03/chapter-0121.md` through `-0130.md` and not against the rest of this state layer.** Ten of Movement 3's thirteen chapters are complete as drafted. **Nobody in this batch is promoted to `bible/characters.md` on usefulness, and the reason is the batch's own argument: a cast is a list of people and this case has spent thirteen weeks refusing lists.** The two who might be argued for — a woman of forty-eight who signs instruments and a woman of fifty-one who types a roll — have earned entries on their own evidence and are recorded here first.

## THE RELATIONSHIPS, ONE LINE EACH, AND NONE OF THEM IS SETTLED

- **Elias / Mara — paying for it, at a price neither of them chose, and the word is still not printed.** **She refused to stop him and said so before he had done it.** On Wednesday 2 September, from a doorway, she told him she was not going to tell him not to, and that she had known about it on Friday the twenty-fifth of July and had said nothing for three days, and that she would like both of those to be in his book and neither of them to be an excuse. On Friday the third she asked him whether she could put his name on a card and he said yes in about nine seconds and gave the reason in about forty words: **because you are the only person in this city who has ever said that the whole of what this case has is a name against a date, and if it is only ever pointed at people who have not asked to be caught then it is a weapon and not an instrument.** She used his reason verbatim and said she was not going to improve on it. On Tuesday 7 September she said, to him and not to the table, **that she was not going to forgive him on Monday and was not going to not forgive him either, and that the reason she was not going to not forgive him is that he asked her to put his name on a card and meant it, and a man who means it has bought the only thing anybody in this case has ever been able to sell anybody.** ⚠ **Neither of them uses the word and the word is not printed in any of the ten chapters, and the batch says so three times in the notebooks, and each of them has now noticed twice in one week that the other does not say it, and neither of them has acted on noticing.**
- **Elias / the professionals — he is in the room and he is the one who is not a party, and it stopped being a compliment this batch.** A housing officer of fifty-eight said in a room on 27 August that a printed sheet with an address box on it breaks a minute she signed, and put her own name at the bottom of it. **On Monday 6 September she refused the seventh of his seven refusals, in about nine words, and it is the only argument anybody has made against him in thirty-eight weeks that he did not already have, and he had it, and he did not have it in the right order: *you are not the second knock, you are the man who put the name in the column, and a knock from you would not be a knock from an office.***
- **Elias / Hesta Rell — six weeks of not being asked anything, and it is the closest two people in this case have come.** She came down a hill three times in six weeks and was asked nothing on the third, and she said in a room, in advance, **that there is a question she has not asked and that when it is asked somebody should know that she said it was not now.** And on the third visit she said one thing about a woman who opened her door in the middle of the night for a stranger, and the thing was **that nobody in this case has walked to a door**, and she said it without any weight on it, and then said that she had telephoned a registrar on the eighth of December 2024 and walked four miles and a man of ninety-one had got onto a page, and that is why she can say it. **It is the moral floor in a different mouth and neither of them has said the sentence and both of them knows the other has it.** She is not the person who refused the list of four and he has not asked for it.
- **Elias / Ivo Pell — the only conversation in the batch that a man of twenty-five came to have.** Ivo is not in the prose of any of the previous ten chapters and he is in two days of this one, and he said what a second person is worth in about nine sentences and then said the thing nobody in the case had said to Elias in thirty-eight weeks: **that being useful and being a party are not opposites, that Elias has decided the practice at Sillick Lane and a flood licence are the same instrument, and that he thinks he is wrong about that.** ⚠ **He asked for it to be minuted by somebody who does not like him and nobody in that room does not like him, and Elias did not write it down, and the reason was that a name in a book is his and a sentence in a room is theirs.**
- **Elias / the woman of about forty-three — he is the man who put a name against her entry and she does not know, and that is the specific cruelty and it is the whole of what is left of him on this.** He did not go to her door. ⚠ **The person he harmed most is not going to know that he did it, and the woman he did harm first is alive, at home, forty-nine years in the same house, and is not angry about it, and both of those are worse than anger.**
- **Elias / Yvette Carrow — the antagonist of the movement in competence and not in intention, and proved right about the bank in the middle of it.** She said the cost out loud twice before she signed, was right four times about the water, signed at 11:41 on Tuesday 10 August, and then found out that the form has a second side. **She was wrong about herself for four days in the correct direction and said so, and a woman of forty-seven from a register told her in a room that she has no power over the block and she believed it, and she said that is the second time anybody has told her that and the first time she believed it.**
- **Elias / the woman of sixty-nine at number twenty-seven — she opened a door for a stranger at half past twelve in the morning on the night of the eighteenth, stood in her hall with it open for about four hours, and nobody has asked her about it, and there is no form, and a duty officer of forty-seven told her on the telephone that there is no form.** She has been waiting nine weeks to be asked. She rang the office herself on the Monday after the strike-through and asked to be asked about the night, and was told there was no place for it, and said she would think about putting it in writing, and did not. ⚠ **She is not a device and she is not a victim in a scene. She asked whether she could give a card back and was told she could not, and she said on the telephone that she was not as upset about that as she would have thought, and she was right.**
- **Elias / the professionals he refuses — unchanged. Hattie Bramm is not asked, again. Cliff Sowerby is not rung.** The silence is thirty-five weeks and it is deliberate on her side and uncomfortable on his, and it is still the only outstanding thing he is aware of that he has chosen to leave.

## DELYTH MORGAN, 47 — duty officer, district register, Northglass. NEW IN THIS BATCH, and she is not a villain.

**Nineteen years in post. She completes the reverse of a flood-emergency access suspension and she has never once been asked to consider whether it is a good idea, because it is not her decision, and she says that in a room and says that she has a job and her job is to complete the block accurately when the front of the form has been signed by somebody with a power.** She brings the register's own guidance note, printed in April, and reads it out in about four minutes without being interrupted. **A status is not a determination. It does not remove anybody from anything that pays anybody. It goes on the frontage, and a frontage is a line on a map, and a line on a map is not a house and is not a person.** She has signed eight of the nine statuses used in this county since 24 June and will sign the ninth on Wednesday 1 September, and she says the arithmetic is very bad during a flood emergency and that the reason is that a frontage with four hundred open claims on it is a frontage a computer will not settle a district boundary against, and everybody who has ever tried has ended up with a district that cannot be drawn.

**On the Friday she entered a living woman's name into a public schedule at about 11:20 in the morning on a man's say-so, in front of about forty people, and she rang the woman at about half past twelve and told her the truth before she asked a question, and she has said in public that she entered it anyway and would not have written the reason on the correction if the man had not stood up at ten past four and said all of it in a room.** She made the correction on Monday 7 September at 10:10, **struck through and not erased, because a schedule of persons provisionally recorded is a record of what people said and the thing struck out is somebody's statement and not the office's certainty.** She says the correction is the first document in this flood that says a person was somewhere else and it took nineteen years of somebody's job to produce it and about nine minutes on a telephone. **And she has said the thing nobody expected a duty officer to say, which is that the letter that was sent to the woman is a record of what the office told her and does not say one word about the door, and that a witness statement is what would say one word about the door, and that there is no form and no place to send it and that she tried it in her head about nine times and it does not go in.**

## INA MORDAUNT, 51 — school business manager, Marlbank Primary. NEW IN THIS BATCH, and she earns it on her own evidence.

**Nineteen years in the building, sixteen of them typing the roll. She has typed every line of it out of an enrolment form a parent filled in when the child was between three and four.** About three hundred and eighty children.

**She refused the roll in about nine seconds on the Wednesday morning and the reason is not modesty: *a school roll does not go anyway. That is the whole of it. It has not gone anywhere in sixteen years. Nobody has asked me for it in sixteen years, not the council, not an inspector, not a police officer, not once, and I have never had to say no, and if I hand it to a room like this one then in about four years there will be a document somewhere that says three hundred and eighty children were on a low bank in June, and it will be a good document, and it will be a document that got made because somebody needed it for something, and nobody will remember what we used it for, and neither will I.*** She says that a school is a building and that the person in the building is the person who decides and that there is nobody else in the building.

**On the Thursday afternoon she did the thing four professionals had told her not to do, having known since about ten past two that she was doing it wrong, and she asked one question at one door of eleven households — *is there anybody at your address who is not on our roll and who we should know about* — and got eleven answers, and four of them are in a drawer in a first-floor office on a pad, and there is no form in nineteen years for a drawer, and a council estates officer told her that the school cannot answer a subject access request because it is not a data controller of anything and that there is a woman of about forty-one in a school in another ward who would know what to tell her and he does not have her name.** ⚠ **She said, in a room, that she had known since ten past two on the Thursday afternoon and did it anyway, and that she had watched about four hundred meetings in the district and knew exactly what happens to a thing that somebody knew was not right. She is not a villain and she is not a hero and she is the person in the building.**

## YVETTE CARROW, 48 — she is proved right about the bank in the middle of the volume, and that is the point of her

**Eleven years in post. She wrote four lines on Monday 4 August and read them out on Tuesday 11 August's predecessor in the room above the shop, having promised herself on Friday 23 July in a car park on the ring road that she would decide in an office and not on a quay.** The four lines are the whole of the case for a second instrument and item three of them is the sentence the whole volume turns on: **there is no printed table in this county that states the level at which water enters the Ferry Bank district behind this wall, and the wall is older than the district's records.** She could not answer the condition a man of fifty-five set her in the car park and she said so in a room, and the reason is that the answer is a man of thirty-two telling a man of fifty-five what happens to a street and the man of thirty-two has no standing to say what happens to a street in this city, and the man of fifty-five knew that, which is why he set it in those words.

**She said the cost out loud twice before she signed, and the second time is worse, and she asked to be stopped and nobody stopped her because she gave them about nine seconds.** She signed at 11:41 on Tuesday 10 August and the office does not initial, and she said out loud twice that an initial is a person acknowledging a document and a document under this Schedule is not a person.

**Then she found out the form has a second side and she is the person who signs both.** ⚠ **She was wrong about herself for four days in the correct direction and said so in a room: she had been asking for an explanation of why it was a bad thing, and nobody in this case was going to be rude to a statutory officer, and a housing officer of fifty-eight had been rude to her about something else in a corridor and she has not stopped thinking about that either.** She says the sentence at the foot of the front of the form — *applications under this Schedule are not persons and are not recorded* — is the most accurate sentence on the form, and that it is a statement about the front, and that it is also a warning addressed to her, and that it says the definition of a person changes on the other side of the piece of paper. **She has been right about the water four times since June and the water has never once been the thing anybody is fighting her about, and she said on Wednesday 1 September that she has started to find that she does not know what to do with being right.** Not a villain, not wrong about the water, and the antagonist of this movement in competence only.

## THE OTHERS, ALL COMPETENT, NONE A VILLAIN, NONE A FOOL

- **Dinah Vassallo, 41** — structural engineer, nineteen years, appointed under the June instrument, four visits since June. Took the 13 mm reading. **Would not guess in front of a wall, and said the finding is the rate and not the number.** Left a surveyor's card in a crack and left it there to find out who else had done the same thing, because a card in a crack is a thing that happens at about two o'clock in the afternoon.
- **Douglas Nairn, 44** — council estates, twenty-two years' worth of a spreadsheet with four hundred and six buildings in it and a level on each. **Could do the join in about nine minutes with a tool that already exists and may not, and the rule is not his and its exceptions are in a schedule in somebody else's office, and he said in about six minutes that he had got nowhere.** Has never once been able to get a person out of a building and has asked three times.
- **Colin Prowse, 63** — caretaker, Marlbank Primary, twenty-nine years, a key to every door, a plumber before he was a caretaker. **Painted a line twenty-three inches off the skirting on a gym wall and wrote the date under it in his own hand on Friday the ninth of July 2025, because he is not daft.** Said that a rule is only a number if somebody knows where the ground is, and that he has asked twice where the floor is and been told it is not his floor.
- **R. Vellacott, about 34** — number sixteen, Ander's Row, eight years in the house. **Wrote two lines at his own kitchen table, and the second one is his and nobody asked him for it, and it is about a child of nine who was in that house every night of the flood and whom he fed a boiled egg on the Wednesday because there was no gas.** He would not let the paper go and said *that is not yours to give to anybody and it is not mine either.* ⚠ **He is not a device and he is not a witness anybody asked for. He is a man who was asked a small thing and gave more than the small thing and then kept it.**
- **Yolanda Speke, 41** — a first floor at 18 Ander's Row, nine years, and her husband has been in a wheelchair for three years and came home from the hospital on the fourteenth of June. **She telephoned an office on 11 August, was told to find somebody with a duty to be the applicant, could not find anybody, and was put in box one of a licence with *none* written in the grade-or-post line.** She signed the only line on the form that is for a person, she asked what it was for, she was told, and she said *that is worth having*, and she wrote the reference on the back of her hand with a biro because she had nothing to write it with. **She had signed for two men in nine years and had never known there was a line to sign on.**
- **Yvonne Petch, 51** — branch manager, twenty-six years, a hall with a table at the end of it and a fire door and a rule about leaflets. Four conditions, one of them that nobody under about sixteen comes to the table. **Would not have thirty-one unknown objects in her corridor and told the room so in about nine seconds and was entirely right, and it is the reason a grey box of twenty-two things with no maker and no date is in a room above a shop in Fisher Lane.**
- **Priscilla Nunn, 66, and Gerald Nunn, 59** — 14 Ander's Row, eleven and twenty-two years, not married, a policy in his name since 2011 because he was the one at the insurance office when they came round. **Both right. He said the water came on the ninth and she said it came on the ninth and came back on the tenth, and the office is right that there is one box and two events.** ⚠ **She came to a door in Low Ward at about half past six on the Saturday evening, would not come in, said *you are the man who holds the box because you have got a door on your mother's shop and no post, and there is nothing in the world that says anybody else may*, and told him what she had come to say, and he did not say the moral floor with four witnesses on a doorstep and has written down that the reason was a room and not a rule.**
- **Rukhsana Iqbal, 47** — resilience coordinator. **Ruled to stop on the Tuesday morning of 2 September 2025 and then ruled not to stop, in that order, and said the first ruling is on the record as a thing that happened.** Sent one sentence from a car and would not put her name on a printed sheet. ⚠ **She said the cost out loud first for the third time in five weeks and this time it was about a rule she had signed herself, and the reason she was right is the reason she is good at this: a form that is about water cannot carry a fact about a person and the person has to come from the other side of a wall about a hundred and forty years old.**
- **Sinead Molloy, 47** — present in Chapter 127's room and silent in it, and still the person who would have to type the September page, and still not going to type a false statement.
- **Hesta Rell, 61** — see the relationships above. **Also: the moral floor in a different mouth, spoken without weight, on Tuesday 7 September, by a woman who has never read this case and has not been asked anything by anybody in six weeks except by a man who has not asked.**
- **Ivo Pell, 25**, **Mara Iles, 28**, **Anja Ward, 53**, **Hazel Cray, 58**, **Marianne Cope, 34**, **Averil Shand, 68**, **Gwilym Petherick, about 55** — see the relationships and the thread list. ⚠ **Anja Ward does not appear in the prose of Batch 0003, and Gwilym Petherick does not appear in the prose of Batch 0003, and the second is a decision and the first is a silence and neither of them is a gap anybody should read as anything.**
- **The woman of about forty-three at 14 Ashgill Row. STILL NOT NAMED, BY DESIGN.** She was in a hall for two hours on Friday 3 September, was asked her name once by a duty officer who had said *I would like your name for the—* and said no pleasantly, and the request was withdrawn immediately and without argument. **She put her hand flat on the table, which nobody taught her, and she asked *is it a good thing to be on it*, and was told that it is a working document, and she said *right*, and she stayed for another half an hour.** She has not been asked anything since June and she has a nine-line letter asking her for a date of loss. ⚠ **What she is for is closed in Chapter 129 and it is not a mystery and it is not a punishment. She is a person who was carried out of a building and who has been on a list of the drowned under another woman's name since Friday morning and is not going to know.**

# CHARACTER STATE — REPAIRS OF 26 SEPTEMBER 2026

**Four character facts in this file were wrong in the prose of Batch 0003 and are corrected here and in the chapters. None of the four is a new fact about a person; all four are the state layer and the chapters now saying the same thing.**

- ⚠ **FENN IS A WOMAN AND HAS NO AGE.** `chapter-0123` used *he* and *his* in the scene where she reads the 1902 card, `chapter-0128` called her *the label-room man* with *a sentence of his own*, and `chapter-0123`'s notebook gave her the only age she is ever given in this manuscript — **a woman of fifty-five — which is not canon and has been removed from the page and replaced with the fact that she has none.** She is **surname only, always**, and the label-room finisher, overnight shift, and the sentence that governs both a rope and a door key is hers. **This file is the authority: `bible/characters.md` gives her no age and neither may a chapter.**
- **Dinah Vassallo, 41, structural engineer** — unchanged as a person, and now named in her own chapter. ⚠ **The batch's logline and notebook had a structural engineer who was not tied to the woman on the page; the notebook now says *Dinah Vassallo, a structural engineer of forty-one*, and the logline clause now says what she does in the scene — she put a telltale on the crack and refused to put a number on it in front of a wall.** She is the same person this file has always described.
- **The river engineer is a man.** ⚠ **`chapter-0127` called him *a woman of fifty-five with a rule* in Hazel Cray's speech, and he is a man in Chapters 121, 122 and 130 and in this file. Corrected in the chapter; no change to him here.**
- ⚠ **DELYTH MORGAN, 47, duty officer, does not supply the address and says so.** **She is the only person in the scene who has a book, and in Chapter 128 she now says out loud that the address of the name she wrote came out of that book in about nine seconds, that the book was right, and that the column does not ask her to check it because the column wants a name and not a person.** `RG/26/6` records it. **She is still not a villain and she is still the one who telephoned Marguerite Sowle herself at about half past twelve, and she is still the one who put a struck-through name on a public schedule at 10:10 on Tuesday 7 September and cannot take it back.**

## THE FIGURES THAT WERE WRONG IN THE PROSE AND ARE NOW RIGHT, BY PERSON

- **The woman of fifty at 12 Ander's Row** wrote the **nineteenth of June** on the printed sheet, not the tenth. **The document is still the chapter's failure and for the same reason: a document cannot say what it does not have, and there is no fourth box.**
- **Marguerite Sowle, 69**, has been waiting **eleven weeks**, not nine, to be asked about a door.
- **The woman of about forty-three at 14 Ashgill Row** said she was not starting on a **Friday** afternoon in a hall, because that is the afternoon she was in one.
- **Averil Shand, 68**, registrar of burials, took **her own** minute by telephone on 6 August 2025 and has thirteen days of it, and the finding in the room is **Mara Iles's**. **Shand has not become a second registrar of this case and the two minutes have not been merged.**
- **Hesta Rell, 61**, has been sitting on her second question for **six weeks and a day**, not six weeks to the day, and she has said in advance that it is not now.
- **Mara Iles, 28**, knew on **Sunday 29 August** that he was going to do the opposite of a knock, and said nothing for three days. **She is not going to tell him not to, she has said so, and she has said she would like both facts in his book and neither of them to be an excuse.** Unchanged as a person.
