# CHARACTER STATE — VOLUME 03, BATCH 0004 (Chapters 131–140), as of Tuesday 12 October 2025

**Verified against `chapters/volume-03/chapter-0131.md` through `-0140.md` and not against the rest of this state layer.** Movement 3 is complete and Movement 4 has seven of its seventeen chapters. **Nobody in this batch is promoted to `bible/characters.md` on usefulness, and the reason is the batch's own argument: a cast is a list of people and this case has spent fourteen weeks refusing lists. Three of the people below earned an entry on their own evidence and are recorded here first, and one of them is the batch's turn.**

## THE RELATIONSHIPS, ONE LINE EACH, AND NONE OF THEM IS SETTLED

- **Elias / Mara — paying for it in a thing that is not trust and has never been said, and Chapter 133 closed a movement without either of them using the word.** `trust(ed|ing|y)?` returns **0** across all ten chapters and the notebooks say three times that the count of the two of them noticing the other one not saying it is now five. **What this batch paid for is refusal, twice, and both refusals cost her something and one of them cost him more than the other.** She refused twice to minute two things: the second question and its non-answer, and the two days in a hardback that nobody in this case is entitled to have printed. **The reason in her own words: a minute is a document and a document is read out to a person who was not in the room, and there are two people on this island who would be read it and neither of them is in this case.** And on 12 October she said in a room that she does not know how she is going to say *you are right, and we cannot use it* about nine hundred times in a street, because a sentence in a street cannot be checked, and there is no form for a second person, and she is not asking for one. **And what he paid: he was offered a river engineer's book face down on a wall and refused it in about four seconds because he has already had one warm thing come out of an object this year and believed it, and he said so out loud, and the man with the rule told him his summer reason was not the reason and was right.**
- **Elias / Hesta Rell — six weeks became seven weeks and five days, and then she asked her own second question and was not answered it, and neither of them has said the other word to anybody.** She came down a hill three times in about eight weeks and on the fourth occasion told a room in advance that there was a question she had not asked and that when it was asked somebody should know she had said it was not now, and eleven days later she asked it. *When this room has finished today, in about four years, and somebody from an office that has been reorganised twice wants to know what this room said, who is it that they ask?* **She gave no answer and said before anybody decided that she was not going to, and the reason is that she has been asked this question three times in her life and every answer she has ever given has been about a person, and a thing that has to be carried by a person is a rota and not a practice.** Four people tried and the four answers were about a document, an office, a signature and a rota. She read the fourth condition out again on a telephone because she asked for it, she walked off at half past four and said she was not going to tell him tonight because she has a bad knee, and she was right. **She is the moral floor in a different mouth about doors and not about houses, said without weight, and neither of them has ever said the sentence and both of them knows the other has it.**
- **Elias / Gwilym Petherick — the inversion, and it is the whole of him.** Six weeks of refusing to ask him, and a decision taken in a room on 6 September not to ask him again, and on 20 September he got off a bus at twenty past eight uninvited and said so before he put a spanner anywhere. **He did not give the table. He offered the book nine times since 15 June and was refused in about four seconds, and he told him the summer reason was not the reason.** He set four marks nobody else can read and refused the object-or-place question for the sixth time in a room instead of a car park, and said the answer will be a number beside a knot in a corridor and nobody will have said anything. **He is not thanked and he did not expect to be and he is not a rescue. He came.**
- **Elias / Delyth Morgan — she is the instrument and she has asked to be asked and she is never asked, and the chapter says that she would rather it were a bad thing she could be asked about.** ⚠ **On 14 September she completed the first record-status on ground anybody in this case can name, in front of four people who asked to watch, and she said the reason out loud twice first, and she entered it accurately, and she ticked a box that carries about seven households nobody in the room could name.** She said the front of the form chooses the frontages and that nobody has ever told the person who signs the front that a status is attached to each address behind the premises, and the block says so on its own face. **She is not a villain, she is right about the computation, and in this batch she is also the first person in the case to say out loud that she would rather be checkable than be right.**
- **Elias / Anja Ward — in a room, and not an ally, and not forgiven, and the hardest thing this batch he did was not ask her anything.** ⚠ **A solicitor sent for her, not this case. She was asked whether the Museum holds records about people who have not been told and said three, in a private series, not in the schedule of collections, not searchable, and that she has known for five years that they should be and has neither destroyed nor produced them. She lost an argument about that in about 1998 in a room on the floor below because she was right.** And then the worst thing in Chapter 139, said correctly: it will not be a belief, it will be a record, and in about forty years somebody will open a file and will not be able to tell that the question was never permitted to be asked. **She said she would rather say *I do not know* in a room of seven than have anybody in this flood believe she has been on our side since June, because she has not. Nobody spoke to her for the rest of the afternoon and that was correct. He noticed he had not asked her anything and wrote it down, and it is the first time in five months that not asking has been the hardest thing he has done.**
- **Elias / Marianne Cope — she asked the other people, and he was not in the room, and that is the relationship this batch moved.** She refuses a sentence twice out loud and does not have it typed; she prints a sheet with three boxes in August and a woman puts four people and a dog into the wrong one and he cannot get the paper back; and in September she stands on a pavement for forty minutes and does not put her hand in a post box. **On 23 and 24 September she is the one who asks eleven households one question at a door, and he is four feet behind her at one of them and not at the other ten, and he asked to come and was allowed exactly one.** She asked a woman of fifty-eight's question back at him in about four seconds — *he is in the room, and a man of seventy is afraid of a piece of paper, and what has that to do with your three not-asks* — and she is right. **And she has eleven addresses in a wallet with dates against them and has not looked one of them up and asked a housing officer to say to her face in about four years in case she turns into the person who puts them in a system.**
- **Elias / Hazel Cray — she is the one who says the thing to him in about nine words and it has now happened twice by two different women who do not know they have said the same thing.** On 6 September: *you are not the second knock, you are the man who put the name in the column.* On 23 September: *one, and you stand behind me, and if anybody asks you a question say you are not the office and do not answer it.* ⚠ **And on 14 and 23 September she is the person who says the things nobody else will: a line is not a door, and a period ends, and a number is not a list, and a rota is not a list, and the difference between a person who is hard to find and a person who has no legal existence, which she has been saying in rooms since 1 September and which a solicitor had written down in July.** She signed three things in this batch: no list of addresses for a year, the destruction of a schedule unrecorded, and the decision not to have the recovery page chased. **She is right about lists and wrong about people and she has said so in a room and she came anyway.**
- **Elias / the woman of about fifty-nine and the man of about seventy — the two people this batch is about who are not in the case at all, and the chapter refuses to join them.** She asked a claims handler of thirty-four what her job was and then whether she liked it, and the answer was one word. He was upstairs and asleep and has not been downstairs since the twentieth of June and he is frightened of a piece of paper because in about four years his wife is going to be alive and somebody is going to ask him about it with nothing in front of him. **He asked to be left alone and was left alone. Neither of them is a device and neither has been asked a second question.**
- **Elias / Ivo Pell — the only person in this batch who is on his side and is also the only one who has counted him.** Ivo asked the one question he was entitled to ask a man with a rule — *who is the second person on a mark* — and got an answer that was worse than he wanted and said so and did not argue. He counted the five people in this flood who have wanted a number out of an instrument and have not got one, proposed nothing, and put the five in a folder to be found in about 2031, and said in a room that there is no minute and that this has been the arrangement for five months. **He is not a party to anything and he keeps a note anyway, and that is the friendship, and neither of them has said the other word.**
- **Elias / the professionals he refuses — unchanged.** Hattie Bramm is not asked. Cliff Sowerby is not rung. The man of fifty-five was not asked and did not need to be. **And nobody in this batch was asked a question by anybody in three weeks and he wrote it down in Chapter 130 and this batch has not corrected it, because he was asked one thing, by a woman of fifty-seven, in the form of *would you like to see this book*, and he said no.**

## THE TURN OF THE BATCH — SHEILA KEARNEY, 57 — a ground floor at 9 Sallyport Row, and she is not a professional and not a villain

**She started a book in June because a man with a clipboard asked her for a date she could not produce and did not say out loud that he had not got it, and she has had four months of it.** An exercise book, sixty pence, four columns, **no heading and no name at the top of it and no names anywhere in it.** Forty-one households by the evening of Tuesday 5 October 2025, and the figure is going to be about four hundred by December, and about nine of the about nine hundred people in this district who have a printed recovery sheet in their house are going to hear about it in the next three weeks.

**She worked the no-names out in about a week and the reason is a dog.** A woman on her street put her sister, her sister's two children and a dog into a box headed *one thing that was in the house and is not now*, on a printed form, in a public hall, in August, because there was nowhere else to put them. She was not there, she heard about it from two people, and she sat on a step for about a week and worked out that the moment a name goes into that book the book is a list, and a list goes somewhere, and about four hundred people have already got a piece of paper saying they were asked for a date and did not get an answer.

**She is doing the only thing anybody in this case has ever asked a person to do, which is write things down, and she is doing it four months early and without anybody's permission, and she brought it in herself.** ⚠ **Nobody in this case may touch it, copy it, circulate it, advise against it or destroy it, and nobody in this case may say no to it, and the second half of that is the harder one and it is a registrar of twenty-eight who said it: if she says no then the woman stops, and if she stops then there is nothing.** The sentence the case now has to say about nine hundred times in a street over three months is *you are right, and we cannot use it*, and it has been said eleven times since 5 July and all forty of the times it was said it was in a room.

**She is not a plot function and she is not the second instrument and she is not a resolution. She is a woman with a bad ankle who is going to be extremely difficult for three months and the case is going to have to be able to say one sentence to her about nine hundred times, and there is no form for it and no second person and nobody is asking for one.**

## GWILYM PETHERICK, about 55 — river engineer. He came, and the coming is the whole of him, and the table did not come with it.

**He came uninvited, unasked for and unannounced, at twenty past eight on Monday 20 September, with a folding rule, a bucket, a spanner and a lump of cement, and he said it out loud before he put a spanner anywhere: if it turns out afterwards that the case asked him, he will put the spanner in the back of the van and drive off and be seen again in about 2031.** He said why he came and none of the reasons was flattery: the second instrument ran out at 23:59 on 31 August, nobody renewed it, there is now four hundred metres of a hundred-and-forty-year-old revetment with nobody's name on it, and he is the only person in the county who walks walls.

**He set four brass survey marks at chainage 0+00, 0+130, 0+260 and 0+390, at 4.031, 4.058, 4.104 and 4.149 above a nail he drove himself in 2016, and nobody in this city can read those numbers except him, and he said so before he set the first one.** A mark a person can read is a mark in a system; a mark in a system is a mark somebody can query; a mark somebody can query is a mark that in about four years gets deleted. **He offered the book face down on the wall and was refused in about four seconds, and he said the summer reason was not the reason and gave the real one himself: the man has already had one warm thing come out of an object this year and believed it, and a woman of sixty-nine has a line through her name on a public schedule because of it.** He said he has offered the book nine times since 15 June and will not ask a tenth time and that the offer stands and there is no deadline on it.

**He refused the object-or-place question for the sixth time since March, in a room with seven people in it instead of a car park**, and added that a registrar of twenty-eight told him to his face on the Monday evening that the gap is not going to be filled by guessing, and that this is a thing he has wanted somebody to say in a room for about six weeks. And he said the whole of what he will say about it: *the knots on a rope are a series of marks and a series of marks is a series of heights, and that is arithmetic, and it does not tell anybody why anybody tied them. And when the number comes out it will be a number beside a knot, in a corridor, and it will be mine, and nobody will have said anything.* **He did not convert a height into a level. The answer remains a number beside a knot in Chapter 146. He is not a rescue and nobody thanked him and he did not expect to be.**

**He and a structural engineer of forty-one have independently arrived at the distinction between a fall and a movement, in different buildings, in the same week, and neither of them has been told about the other, and a man of thirty-two noticed and wrote it down and did not introduce them.**

## CATRIONA LYLE, 50 — partner, Lyle, Sadler & Barrow, third floor, Lantern Row. NEW IN THIS BATCH, and she earns it on her own evidence.

**She wrote four clauses on a schedule and attached them to a covering note dated 7 October 2025, privileged, sent to the client's officer alone, no copy made and none to be made, and the advice is dated the seventh and the instrument it concerns does not exist and has not been drafted.** She read it out in a room on a hill in about eleven minutes and then said that she will not explain it, because six of the seven people in the room are not her client and she has sent it to a statutory officer and the officer has done what she likes with it.

**The four clauses are correct and Chapter 139 does not have anybody say that they are right.** Clause four is the whole of her and she said so herself: it is the part she would ask the reader to keep. And she said, in a room, that clause four had been drafted on Tuesday, that a structural engineer said the same sentence on a wharf on Monday, and that she would like it minuted that the two were not the same, **because in about four years two professionals are going to say they were obviously the same and she would like a piece of paper somewhere that says a woman of fifty noticed the collision and did not cause it.**

**She asked the only person in the room who is not a party to anything what the fourth clause does to a person, and wrote the answer in her own book and did not give it to him**, and gave the reason: a person who has an instrument handed to him by a solicitor in a room on a hill has a document, and about nine hundred people in this flood have been handed a document by an office and it was the worst thing that happened to them. **She is right about the land and nobody has asked her about the people, and she is not going to be, and that is her position and it is not a villain's and not a saint's.**

## THE OTHERS, ALL COMPETENT, NONE A VILLAIN, NONE A FOOL

- **Winnie Okoro, 52** — postwoman, eleven years on the emptying round for a pillar box in Wharf Street, a rota that is not a rota of the case's. **She read the front of all six envelopes with the office's reference on them and put a rubber band round the six and not round the other thirteen, and said a rubber band is not a plan.** She came to a room above a shop and said *I have not come to give you anything* before she sat down, and said that a pillar box is not a postal address and that there is no such thing as posting to one, and that a reference on the front of a piece of paper is a thing a machine reads. **She telephoned the office on 21 September, was put through twice, rang back after the second post twice, and came in person on the Monday because a rubber band is not a plan. She is not a record and she does not want to be one and she would have said no to a form in June and gave the reason.** She is in no file and is in nobody's list and is the best-placed person in this flood and has no standing whatever.
- **Cerys Price, 29** — claims assistant, eleven months in post, did a data protection course in July. **She asked the only question that mattered, in about four seconds, and it is the question the whole of Chapter 138 turns on: *who is the data controller*.** If it comes into the office, the office becomes the data controller of forty-one households' recollections of the eighteenth of June, and every one of those households can in writing ask what is held and be answered in about a fortnight, and the office would have to say which bit is theirs. **If it does not come in, it is a woman's exercise book in a ground floor and nobody in the city is responsible for it, including her, and she said that is worse and that she is not being clever.**
- **Helen Venn, 58** — owner of the laundrette in Low Ward, and the first person in this batch to be in a chapter by name. **She was asked, at about four o'clock on 12 October behind the machines, to stand in a room in the week of Monday the twenty-fifth and read back anything he says for three days — Monday the twenty-fifth, Tuesday the twenty-sixth and Wednesday the twenty-seventh of October 2025 — and she said yes before he had finished the sentence, and did not ask why, and gave three words and a bad reason — he has not asked her for one thing since he was fourteen.** She turned a machine off before she answered, which is the first thing about her in the chapter. She said that if he has got anything to say that is not on the board he will have to say it to her face and not to a person with a duty. ⚠ **She is the second person the cost of Chapter 144 requires, she is arranged, and nobody in this case knows what she has been asked to do, and a later batch must not let anybody in the case ask her.**
- **Catriona Lyle** — see above. **Anja Ward** — see the relationships above. **Vera Pilling, 81** — the register's keeper, in two chapters and the only person in this case about whom nothing happened; entry 141, on about the hundred and ninth page of about a hundred and fifty, about forty-one pages left, about four hundred more things to come, and a fourth line on the first page that is the only line in the book addressed to anybody. **She is not a keeper of anything in the sense of a person and she has never been given a role and she has said in front of nine people that she is not going to be asked, and she was not asked, and the only words she heard all day on the first of October were two words from a man of thirty-two and neither of them was a question.**
- **Sinéad Molloy, 47** — typist, twenty-two years, has typed every publication out of that office since 2019. **She refused to type *the City Flood Office has consulted with the residents* and gave the better reason: the sentence would be true when typed and false by the time anybody looked at it, and the only person who did that would be a clerk of forty-seven working late.** She then said, unprompted, that the two best arguments anybody in this flood has got for anything have both come from the people whose whole job is the typing and the saying of a form, and did not know that the man in the room writes everything down and has written it down twice.
- **A duty officer of about thirty-one with nine years** — Delyth Morgan's replacement, who came into Chapter 133's room on a Saturday because a duty officer's rota has no Saturdays in it, to say that a status cannot be appealed because it cannot be, and that she has nothing to add. **She is not a villain and she is not a substitute for anybody and she exists because a register has seven days.**
- **A man of about thirty-four at number 8 on Sillick Lane** — made a typescript copy of the five lines on the wall in about eleven minutes one evening in May because he thought people wanted to know what the wall said, and its fifth line is wrong, and it is staying wrong, and nobody has been asked to take it down and a registrar of twenty-eight has said in a hall that she is not going to. **He is not a villain and he did not know and nobody has explained it to him and nobody is going to in this volume.**
- **A woman of about fifty-four at a ground floor on the low bank** — nineteen years in the house, asked a claims handler what her job was and then whether she liked it, gave five people and a dog and a man from the gas board who stood in her doorway for an hour, and a date she has had since 19 June. **She is the one person in this batch who is not asked a question about the flood in the whole of it and she got two questions she did not ask for, and she is not a device and she is not a witness anybody wanted.**
- **A man of about seventy at the second door** — see the relationships above. **He is the best frightened person in the volume so far and he is frightened of a piece of paper, and he said *I am not frightened of you* and wanted that said.**
- **Mara Iles, 28; Ivo Pell, 25; Hesta Rell, 61; R. Iqbal, 47; Y. Carrow, 48; Yvette's instrument; R. Vellacott, about 34; N. Braithwaite, 58; D. Morgan, 47; H. Cray, 58; M. Cope, 34; D. Nairn, 44; D. Vassallo, 41; C. Prowse, 63; Y. Petch, 51; A. Osei, 36; P. Nunn, 66; G. Nunn, 59; E. Sowerby, 91, of The Paddock, no memorial to be provided, family cannot** — see the relationships and the thread list. ⚠ **Anja Ward is now in the prose of Volume 03, and the house is quiet about that and the silence is not a gap.**
- **The woman of about forty-three at a ground floor in Bankfoot. STILL NOT NAMED, BY DESIGN.** ⚠ **She is referred to twice in this batch — in Chapter 136 by a registrar of twenty-eight and in the Chapter 136 notebook — as a woman of about forty-three who was put on a schedule in a hall and has never been asked, and that is all she is, and no street and no address and no name is given, and she has a nine-line letter from the office on the north side asking her for a date of loss and she is not in any of the ten chapters in person.** She is not this volume's turn and she is not a device and she is the second person the correction of Chapter 130 was not enough for.

# CHARACTER STATE — REPAIRS OF 26 SEPTEMBER 2026 (Volume 03, Batch 0004)

**An independent review found twenty-five findings across the ten chapters. Fourteen were real and all fourteen are repaired. Four of them are character facts and they are here because a state layer that does not carry a correction is a state layer that will hand the next writer the wrong person.**

- ⚠ **WINNIE OKORO, 52, IS THE PERSON WHO EMPTIES THE PILLAR BOX IN WHARF STREET, AT SIX, ON A ROUND, WITH A KEY AND A BAG, AND SHE HAS DONE IT FOR ELEVEN YEARS. She is not a typist and she never was.** The batch's first draft had **Sinead Molloy, 47**, saying *I empty that box at about six* and the narrative saying the Friday collection was done by *a woman of forty-seven who had a key for it*, which contradicted Chapter 133's *a member of the public who holds a key and is not in this case* and then contradicted Winnie Okoro walking into a room in Chapter 136 and saying she has done the job for eleven years. **The typist has been removed from the round and now says that the round is somebody's job and not anybody's duty, and that the person on the round is on it on a Friday and not on a Saturday, and that nobody in this case has her name.** **Sinead Molloy is a typist of forty-seven who has typed every publication out of that office since 2019, who refused to type a false sentence, and who found out on a pavement that a pillar box is not a postal address. She does not empty anything.**
- ⚠ **THE WOMAN WITH THE EXERCISE BOOK IS FIFTY-SEVEN, NOT FIFTY-TWO.** The batch's first draft had a Chapter 139 sentence about *a woman of fifty-two on a low bank with an exercise book with forty-one households in it*, and the woman of fifty-two is the postwoman. **Sheila Kearney is 57**, she is the keeper of the second book, and she is the turn of the batch. The distinction matters because the two women are on the same page in the same volume doing opposite things: one holds six envelopes she is not allowed to hand over, and one holds forty-one households she is not allowed to be told about, and **neither of them is a record and neither of them may be made into one.**
- ⚠ **THE WOMAN WITH THE EXERCISE BOOK HAS NO NAMES IN IT, AND NOW THERE ARE NO NAMES ON THE PAGE EITHER.** The batch's first draft printed *me, Tom, my mother, Kieran 14, Nuala 11* in the photocopy she brought to a counter and *Mrs Enid Prosser* on the next line, and then said in the same chapter and in two notebook blocks that there is not one name in the book. The quoted lines now read *me, my husband, my mother, a boy of 14 and a girl of 11* and *the woman next door*. **The point of the chapter is that she worked the no-names out in about a week and the reason is a dog, and that point is now true on the page and not only in the narrator's hand.**
- ⚠ **THERE IS ONE HOUSING OFFICER OF FIFTY-EIGHT IN THIS CASE AND SHE WAS SPEAKING.** The batch's first draft had her say *on Monday a woman of fifty-eight said the only argument anybody in this case has made against you in forty-one weeks was nine words, and I am not the same woman and I do not know who is*, in a sentence in which she is the woman of fifty-eight. **Rewritten: there is another set of nine words in a corridor about three weeks ago, it was not her, she has never asked who said it and she is not going to now.** Hazel Cray is the housing officer for the district, twenty-two years, and she is the person who says the three most useful sentences in the batch and she has never once been a second housing officer.
- **UNCHANGED AS PEOPLE.** A man of about seventy at the second door is frightened of a piece of paper and is not frightened of the case, and a woman of about fifty-nine asked a claims handler of thirty-four what her job was and then whether she liked it, and **they are not the same thing and the chapter says so.** Gwilym Petherick came uninvited and did not give the table. Catriona Lyle wrote four correct clauses and refused to explain them to six people who are not her client. Anja Ward holds three records about people who have not been told and is right and is not an ally. Vera Pilling has about forty-one pages left in a hardback and nobody asked her what the four hundred were for. Helen Venn said yes before he had finished the sentence. **None of the fourteen repairs changed what any of them wants or is frightened of, and that is the test a repair pass has to pass and it is recorded here because it did.**

# CHARACTER STATE — VOLUME 03, BATCH 0005 (Chapters 141–150), as of Tuesday 16 November 2025. VOLUME 03 IS COMPLETE.

**Verified against `chapters/volume-03/chapter-0141.md` through `-0150.md` and not against the rest of this state layer. Movement 4 is complete and so is Volume 03. Nobody in this batch is promoted to `bible/characters.md` on usefulness, for the batch's own reason, which is that a cast is a list of people and this case has spent twenty-one weeks refusing lists. Four people earned an entry on their own evidence and are written out in full, and one of them is the volume's turn, and one of them is not a person in this case at all.**

## THE RELATIONSHIPS, ONE LINE EACH, AND NONE OF THEM IS SETTLED, AND A VOLUME HAS NOW CLOSED WITHOUT THE WORD

- **Elias / Mara — first reliable in practice and not one word for it, and both of them have now paid for it in public, in different rooms, in the same fortnight.** **The word is not printed in any of the ten chapters, `trust(ed|ing|y)?` returns 0, and Chapter 150 says on the page that a volume has never closed with it and this one is not going to be the first.** What she paid: **a minute.** She had refused twice, in July and in September, to minute a thing because a minute is a document and a document is read out to a person who was not in the room, and on the ninth of November she wrote the first minute this case has had since the fifth of July and it names one person to be asked about one meeting, and that person is him, and he has been told, and she wrote down that she has looked for something that needs him and did not find one and gave him a tray of paper instead. What he paid: **a sentence he had ready and did not say**, in a room on Wharf Street on the Monday morning of the fifteenth, to a woman of sixty-six who said the thing he has been carrying since the second of June better than he could have said it in five months, and he did not say it because it was not his and had not been since June, and because she said it first and better, and he has written down that in September the not-asking was the hardest thing he had done in five months and that this was not hard, and that this is the thing he is taking somewhere.
- **Elias / Helen Venn — she is his mother, she is fifty-eight, she owns the laundrette on the low bank, and she is both the instrument and the check, and he has said so on the page and has not forgiven her and has not blamed her and is not going to either.** She said yes before he had finished the sentence on 12 October and gave three words and a bad reason and it was hers. On 26 October she said a date out loud, kindly, in a room with about nine people in it, and got the order of two rows wrong, and nothing whatever in it except that. On 28 October she read five days of his notebook back and found nothing wrong in it and could not find anything wrong in it, and said she had been the second person for a thing since September and did not know that was what it was going to be. On 12 November he read about two hundred households out of her book in a front room and afterwards could not tell her whether he had read them in her order, and told her, because she is the second person and the second person is supposed to be told. **He did not ask her a second time about a day book on a hill on 15 November and gave four reasons and the third one is that he is not going to spend her twice in a fortnight on the same case.** ⚠ **Their relationship is the only one in this volume that is load-bearing and it is broken on the page and nobody in the case knows.**
- **Elias / Ivo Pell — the friendship, unchanged, and the friendship is a folder in a drawer dated 2031.** He put a note on the corner of it on 29 October 2025 about a thing nobody had thought about, which is that this case has been keeping its memory in one man since the sixth of December and that the failure is not a person and does not look like a break. He has not been thanked. He is not a party to anything and he keeps a note anyway, and he told the man of thirty-two to go and read a day book in a building on a hill on a Monday because he is on at ten and the man of thirty-two is not, and he has not asked him anything.
- **Elias / Gwilym Petherick — the inversion, complete.** Six weeks of refusing to ask him, a decision on 6 September not to ask him again, and then he came twice, uninvited, and said so at the door both times, and gave away the only conversion in the county on 3 November, unasked, because a man called Ferris telephoned him at eight o'clock, and said no, in a corridor, to the one question anybody asked him, and wrote a number on a piece of paper and gave it away and said nothing. **He was asked once in three months and he said no and nobody improved on it and the answer is a number and not a sentence.** He also said, standing up, that the wrongness in him is not the rope and is not the knots and is that he called a laminated sheet a conversion for five months because calling it a conversion is what kept him from being asked to hand it over.
- **Elias / Hazel Cray — she is the sentence of the volume's best administrative chapter and she says it in a corridor and not in a room, and the reason is that a person who is the only one in a room has to be looked after and this one has been looked after for nineteen weeks and nobody has ever once asked him whether he would like it.** She signed the instrument on 5 November and said out loud that she did not know what to do with being right. She is right about lists, wrong about people, and has said so in a room, and came anyway. And on 19 October she said a woman's name out loud in a doorway in about four seconds because she was pleased to be able to help somebody standing in front of her, and said afterwards that the rule is that nobody touches that book, and went to tell the woman the same afternoon.
- **Elias / Anja Ward — six weeks of not asking, and it stopped being the hardest thing, and he has written down that it stopped and has not written down what replaced it.** She was not in the room on 5 November, was not told about the seal, and is not going to be told about the register's reply. She holds three records about people who have not been told. She said in October that what clause four builds is not a belief, it is a record, and a man of fifty-five in a corridor on 12 November said he has thought about her sentence every day since and is not going to improve on it, and has never met her.
- **Elias / Anja Ward and the volume's last silence — recorded because the volume closes on it.** *It will not be a belief. It will be a record.* Nobody spoke to her for the rest of the afternoon on 7 October and that was correct. On 5 November she was not in the room. On 12 November a man of fifty-five said he was not going to improve on her. On 16 November a man of thirty-two did not ask a Director anything for the sixth week running and wrote it down and the word is not printed.
- **Elias / the woman of fifty-seven — the only relationship in the volume that starts as a refusal and ends as a question, and it is the wrong way round and he knows it.** She brought a book to a counter in October and he said no in about four seconds. He read it in a front room in November after asking, and gave it back, and told her afterwards that he could not check the order of it. **Nobody in this case may touch that book and nobody may say no to it, and the one person in the case who has read it did so at her own table and copied nothing and advised against nothing and told her the truth about himself afterwards, and that is the whole of what he can be to her and it is not nothing.**
- **Elias / the registrar of twenty-eight — she can do without him in four days, and she wrote it in her own book, and she told him in a corridor that she was not going to ask a question she was asked in a car park in September, and neither of them has said the word and the volume closed on that.**
- **Elias / Hesta Rell — her question is still open, she is still not asked, and neither of them has said anything.** Nine weeks in the case and eleven since the second question. **He has noticed nine times that he has not asked her and has written the number down because somebody has to keep a count of the noticing as well as a count of the not-asking, and she said in June that she was not going to be asked and has held to it, and the chapter that closed her movement said nobody was going to answer her and it is still true in November.**

## THE TURN OF THE BATCH — PRISCILLA NUNN, 66 — 14 Ander's Row, and she is not a professional and not a device

**She is the answer to the question the whole volume has been asking and she is not Elias Venn and she is not a registrar and she is a woman of sixty-six with a carrier bag and four things in it.** She has been to four offices since the second of July and every one of them told her that the date in the fourth box is worth nothing to her, and all four were right, and she worked the arithmetic out on a bus in the second week of July.

> **9 OR 10 DEC**

**A date of loss is for seven hundred and fifty pounds once per household per event. That is all it is. It is not for the file and it is not for the truth and it is not for finding out what happened to my sister, and it is not even for the twelve pounds fifty, it is for the seven hundred and fifty, and I did not claim the excess, so my sister has no date and the date was worth nothing to me.**

**She said it out loud on the morning of Monday the fifteenth of November in a room on Wharf Street, before about nine people, and she said it because about nine hundred people in this district are going to be told something before Christmas and there is no form for it and no leaflet and nobody will sign one, and she wanted the woman who is going to say it to her mother in a kitchen to have heard it said by somebody who lost something by hearing it. Nobody improved on it. A man of thirty-two was in the room and had it ready and did not say it.**

She has a sewing machine in her front room that belongs to her sister, a piano in a removals van in a street off a low bank, a wedding hamper, and seventeen hundred and forty pounds on an itemised list. She has been eleven years at that address and her brother twenty-two. **Nobody in this case has been asked about the van and nobody has been told where the piano is and it is not coming back this year.** On 4 November she came into a room and was given her sister's name and a date and a place of burial and stood in the room for about four minutes and then went out and sat in a corridor, and a housing officer of fifty-eight went out with her and came back in and said she was all right and that she had asked for a sheet of paper with the entry on it and had wanted that more than she wanted anything anybody in this case has ever offered her. **There is no piano in that corridor.**

## GILYM PETHERICK'S STATE AT THE END OF THE VOLUME, AND IT IS COMPLETE

He came uninvited on 20 September and said so before he put a spanner anywhere, and he came again uninvited on 12 November and said so at the door, and **both times he was right that a man who came when he was asked is a different man.** He set four brass marks nobody else can read and refused the object-or-place question for the sixth time in September and **the seventh time in a corridor on 12 November, standing up, because he had decided he was not going to say it sitting down in a room like the one in June.** He did not convert a height into a level. **On 3 November he took the laminated sheet out of a buttoned pocket in a corridor and read one line of it and let a registrar of twenty-eight write ten numbers on a second sheet, and gave the sheet away, and put the laminated sheet back and buttoned the pocket, and said nothing for the rest of the day.** He was not asked. A man called Ferris telephoned him at eight o'clock in the morning and told him a rope had been brought off a hose hook and did not make a thing of it. **He has been offered the book nine times since 15 June and refused in about four seconds every time and the offer stands and there is no deadline on it. He and a structural engineer of forty-one have independently got the distinction between a fall and a movement, in different buildings, and neither has been told about the other, and the man of thirty-two has noticed and has still not introduced them and has now noticed it four times.**

## THE OTHERS, ALL COMPETENT, NONE A VILLAIN, NONE A FOOL, AND NONE OF THEM ASKED

- **Wes Lockhart, about 24, eleven weeks in post** — night relief at the front desk of a building on a hill. He wrote three lines in a day book that is the record of issue and return, one line per device, and the third line has no date on it and says that nobody had put him on the rota, that he has asked at the desk and been told there was no rota, that he is on there the nights and does not know who is putting him on there, and that he has not told anybody that he does not know and is writing it down because this is the book they tell you to write it in. **He is not a supernatural event and he is not a device and he is a person on a rota that does not exist, and a member of the public read his line and did not ask him about it.**
- **A records officer of about fifty at Northglass** — asked one question in June and refused, asked again in September and refused, **and telephoned a room in Fisher Lane on the afternoon of Tuesday the ninth of November having been asked nothing at all, and said in about nine seconds that in about four years somebody is going to run a query for a registration district called Ferry Bank and there is not going to be one, and they are not going to be lying.** She was right, and it happened on the twelfth, and she has not been asked about it and has not been thanked and is not going to be. **She is the only job in this flood that nobody has ever written a novel about, and she said so in a room and it was not a joke.**
- **Ferris** — about nineteen years on that bank, has had the rope on a hose hook at South Sluice and has apologised for a wall for four years, and telephoned a river engineer at eight o'clock on Wednesday morning and said it in about four seconds and would not have said it if he had not been asked. **He is not a villain and he is not a rescue and he has never been in this case and nobody has his name in a file.**
- **A conservator of about thirty-eight** — took the one photograph of the reach rope in this case, on a wet table in a lock yard on Wednesday the ninth of June 2025, with a telephone, because the thing on the table was the only object anybody in this case had wanted to photograph. **She did not know at the time that she was photographing an instrument and thought she was photographing a curiosity, and the picture was printed on ordinary office paper at the size it is and put on a wall with no caption and no maker in the maker's line, and it turned out on 3 November to be the thing that read an order a man of thirty-two could not give, eleven weeks after it went up.**
- **N. Braithwaite, 58** — adopted 6 March 2011, in force since 2009, Part 9, domestic and commercial. **Asked in writing in July whether the section is fit for the purpose it is being used for, and has not answered, and it is eleven weeks.** He is not a villain and nobody in this case is going to make him one, he has not been asked a second time, no instrument in this case can require him to answer anything, and the reason nobody has written again is that a reissue makes about four hundred and eleven wallets out of date and about nine hundred people in this district are going to be paid or not paid out of that box before anybody in the country knows what he thinks of it. **He is not in the room on 5 November and was not asked about the seal.**
- **Catriona Lyle, 50** — the four clauses are in the instrument and it is signed and dated. She wrote them in July, she read them out on 7 October, she asked the only person in the room who is not a party to anything what the fourth clause does to a person, and she is not in this batch and has not been asked about the people. **She is right about the land and nobody has asked her about the people and she is not going to be.**
- **A man of about forty-one with a clipboard and nothing on it** — nineteen years in the job, went down about nine streets on the morning of Saturday 6 November and told about nine houses that a piece of paper exists which says every person at an address continues to be described as present at that address for every purpose this office can think of. **He read the number of the instrument out loud twice from memory and it was right both times. He wrote nothing down. He was not asked for anything and gave nothing to anybody, and there is no form for what he did and he said so.**
- **Prudence Halloway, 74** — the freeholder. Eleven miles away. Her agent came on 1 November with a file and had been in the building once before the flood and has said so. **She has not been in the building and was not asked to be.**
- **A licensed officer of thirty-six with eleven years** — broke a bond in about nine minutes in the rain on 1 November, would not let anybody help her, would not let the agent do the schedule on a nine-minute timetable, and said that the thing she was about to do was the only thing in her week she was not allowed to do badly. **She is not a villain and she is not a rescue and she cut a shutter once, in June, and refused to cut it twice.**
- **Vera Pilling, 81** — the keeper. About two hours on **Thursday 12 November**, in her own kitchen, with the book on the table and the hairband on the table, and she turned one page that was under his hand, the way you do it, and that was the whole of the assistance. **She said about four hundred more things to come, and it has not moved since about the eleventh of July, and she said it because she wanted one person in this city to have been told, and she has had five people in that kitchen in five weeks and not one of them has asked her a question because they have all been told not to, and she was not asked one in twenty weeks and was not going to be.**
- **Sheila Kearney, 57** — about two hundred households, no heading, no name, no names, sixty pence, a bag under a crockery board. **She telephoned the office on 8 November because the office's number is on a printed sheet in about nine hundred houses, and she asked whether she is still not to bring it in, and she did not ask it as a favour and she did not ask it because she has doubts about it.** She is going to be extremely difficult for three months and nobody in this case is permitted to say no to her.
- **A man of about seventy at the second door, and a woman of about fifty-nine at number six, and a woman of about thirty-four who worked a door round in October, and a man of about thirty-four at number sixteen with a drawer** — none of them was asked a question in this batch and none of them is in any of the ten chapters. **A woman of sixty-nine is alive and at home. A woman of about forty-three is on a public schedule and has never been asked her name. A girl of nine is in a piece of paper in a drawer that no office in this city can take.**
- **Mara Iles, 28; Ivo Pell, 25; Hesta Rell, 61; R. Iqbal, 47; Y. Carrow, 48; R. Vellacott, about 34; D. Nairn, 44; D. Vassallo, 41; C. Prowse, 63; A. Osei, 36; S. Molloy, 47; A. Ward, 53; C. Lyle, 50; G. Petherick, about 55; H. Cray, 58; M. Cope, 34; H. Venn, 58; V. Pilling, 81; S. Kearney, 57; N. Braithwaite, 58; P. Nunn, 66; D. Morgan, 47** — see the relationships and the thread list. **Anja Ward is now in the prose of Volume 03 in three chapters and is not an ally in any of them and has not been forgiven and has not been asked anything for six weeks.**

# CHARACTER STATE — VOLUME 03 CLOSE, 2026-09-26. THE AUDIT, THE PROMOTION DECISION, AND WHAT CHANGES

**Read against all fifty chapters of Volume 03 and not against the rest of this file. Volume 03 is closed. This section is the volume-local record's last entry and it is also the decision about what goes into the bible.**

## THE PROMOTION DECISION, MADE ONCE, AND IT IS THE HOUSE RULE FOR VOLUMES 04 THROUGH 12

**Nothing is promoted out of Volume 03 except what was already promoted. The residents of Sillick Lane, Helen Venn, Sheila Kearney, Priscilla Nunn, Gwilym Petherick, Catriona Lyle, W. Lockhart, and the four professionals promoted out of Volumes 01 and 02 STAY VOLUME-LOCAL, in this file, and do not go into `bible/characters.md`'s main cast table.**

**Vera Pilling is the one exception and she was made in Volume 02, on her own evidence and not on her usefulness**, and her row says *a keeper is not a leader, do not let her be given one*, and the exception is the only promotion this manuscript has ever made and the reason for it had nothing to do with being useful.

**The reason, and it is the manuscript's own argument and it is twenty-one weeks old:** **a cast is a list of people, and this case has spent twenty-one weeks refusing lists.** Five batches, five refusals, one exception. A list of houses is worse than a list of people and a bible cast is a list of people. **What is inherited forward is the street, the building, the office and the written position, and not a roll of the people standing in it.**

**The mechanism that replaced refusal as a rule, so that the rule can be applied rather than repeated:** a new volume's working professionals go into a **volume-scoped table** in `bible/characters.md` — the pattern *Volume 01 Working Cast* and *Volume 03 Working Cast* already set. **A person with a life outside the case stays in this file. A person a later volume may want badly enough to be manufactured into a motive stays in this file too.** And **nobody is promoted out of a volume because a later volume wants them**, which is the whole rule and it is one sentence.

## THE RELATIONSHIPS AT THE CLOSE OF VOLUME 03, ONE LINE EACH, AND NONE OF THEM IS SETTLED

- **Elias / Mara — first reliable in practice and not one word for it, and both of them paid for it in public, in different rooms, in the same fortnight.** **The word is not printed in any of the ten chapters, `trust(ed|ing|y)?` returns 0, and Chapter 150 says on the page that a volume has never closed with it and this one is not going to be the first.** What she paid: **a minute** — the first this case has had since 5 July, written on 9 November, naming one person to be asked about one meeting, and that person is him, and he has been told, and she wrote down that she has looked for something that needs him and did not find one and gave him a tray of paper instead. What he paid: **a sentence he had ready and did not say**, to a woman of sixty-six who said it better than he could have said it in five months, and he has written down that this was not hard and that it is the thing he is going to have to take somewhere. **She can do without him in four days, has written that down in her own book, and has told nobody except him, in a corridor.**
- **Elias / Helen Venn — she is his mother, fifty-eight, she owns the laundrette on the low bank, and she is both the instrument and the check.** Yes before he had finished the sentence; a date said out loud kindly in a room with about nine people in it, getting the order of two rows wrong; five days read back in about half an hour finding nothing wrong and being unable to find anything wrong; and told afterwards that he could not check the order of a book he had just read in her own front room. **It is the only load-bearing relationship in the volume and it is broken on the page and nobody in the case knows.**
- **Elias / the woman of fifty-seven (Sheila Kearney, 9 Sallyport Row) — the only relationship that starts as a refusal and ends as a question, and it is the wrong way round and he knows it.** He said no to her book in about four seconds in October; he read it in her own front room in November after asking, gave it back, copied nothing, advised against nothing, and told her afterwards that he could not check the order of it. **She said she was not going to be able to check that either, and she was right, and nobody is.**
- **Elias / Hesta Rell, 61 — seven weeks and five days in the case, from 26 July to 18 September 2025, and not asked since 18 September 2025, which is fifty-nine days at the close of the volume — the figure of fifty-two in the Batch 0005 record is correct for Chapter 148 and is on the page there.** Her first question was *Is a place allowed to say no*, six words, at about ten past four on Monday 26 July. Her second was asked in Chapter 133 and answered by nobody, and she said in advance that it was not now. **The interval between the two is seven weeks and five days, and the manuscript says six weeks and a day in two chapters and one of them also puts the first in June. Recorded in `state/continuity.md`; not repaired.**
- **Elias / the man of fifty-five (Gwilym Petherick, river engineer) — the inversion, complete.** Six weeks of refusing to ask him; a decision on 6 September not to ask again; and then he came twice, uninvited, said so at the door both times, and gave away the only conversion in the county on 3 November because a man on a pump floor telephoned him at eight o'clock. He said no, in a corridor, to the one question anybody asked him. **He is asked once in three months and nobody improves on it, and the answer is a number and not a sentence.**
- **Elias / the man of fifty-five and the woman of fifty-eight — the two sentences in this volume that a later volume will try to make into one.** Petherick: *it will be a number beside a knot in a corridor and nobody will have said anything.* Cray, in a corridor on 29 October, not in a room: **we do not need him, and that is not a result, and we got there in four days, and he is the only person in this case who can hold a thing and we are not going to ask him to, and both of those are true and only one of them is allowed to be in a file, and a person who is the only one in a room has to be looked after and this one has been looked after for nineteen weeks and nobody has ever once asked him whether he would like it.**
- **Elias / Ivo Pell, 25 — the friendship, unchanged, and the friendship is a folder in a drawer dated 2031.** He put a note on the corner of it on 29 October 2025: this case has been keeping its memory in one person since 6 December and the failure is not a person and **it does not look like a break, it looks like a competent man.** He is not a party to anything and he keeps a note anyway, and he told the man of thirty-two to go and read a day book on a Monday because he is on at ten and the man of thirty-two is not.
- **Elias / Hazel Cray, 58, housing officer, twenty-two years — right about lists and wrong about people, and she has said so in a room, and she came anyway.** She signed the seal on 5 November and said out loud that she did not know what to do with being right. She said the sentence of Chapter 145 in a corridor because a room with him in it is a room where a person has to be looked after. And on 19 October she said a woman's name out loud in a doorway in about four seconds because she was pleased to be able to help somebody standing in front of her, and went to tell the woman the same afternoon. **There is one housing officer of fifty-eight in this case and she is her.**
- **Elias / Yvette Carrow, 48, the office that issues a flood-emergency access suspension — right about the water four times and never once about the people, and she has started to find that she does not know what to do with being right.** She is proved right at the midpoint and the proving is the catastrophe.
- **Elias / Anja Ward, 53 — six weeks of not asking, and it stopped being the hardest thing, and he has written down that it stopped and has not written down what replaced it.** Not in the room on 5 November, not told about the seal, not going to be told about the register's reply. *It will not be a belief, it will be a record.* ⚠ **AND THE ONE THING A VOLUME 04 WRITER NEEDS ABOUT HER THAT THE AUDIT COULD NOT FIND ANYWHERE IN THE PLAN: she is the hand on the only instrument that closes the fourth level.** *Preservation Order No. 3 of 2026*, one page, three clauses, made and served and executed on pinned Thursday 12 May 2026 by the Quiet Accord Registry in her two capacities, on the ground of a gas risk that is real. **She is not told what a district register said in four lines in November 2025 and she never will be, and she can hold an instrument that closes a floor without ever learning that, and no chapter may put those two facts in the same head — that is the whole of her in this volume and it is already in her main cast row: *her willingness to treat certain people as acceptable losses is also real*, arriving on a date, in her own hand, with a schedule attached.** She locks the west-range stair door herself.
- **Elias / the man of twenty-five's finding, and the registrar of twenty-eight's sentence, and the man of thirty-two's silence — these three are one thing and they are what a Volume 04 writer inherits instead of a plot.** A man of twenty-five put a note in a folder to be found in about 2031; a registrar of twenty-eight wrote a minute and a card and has still not answered a question she was asked in a car park in September; and a man of thirty-two did not say a sentence he had ready in a room full of people. **All three are the same sentence in three mouths and none of them has been said to the person it is about.**

Marianne Cope is thirty-four and is the claims handler. Thirty-four governs.** Not repaired in the chapter; recorded for a repair pass.
- ⚠ **THE VOLUME'S CENTRAL SENTENCE IS SAID BY ELIAS VENN AND NOT BY A REGISTRAR OF TWENTY-EIGHT.** `chapter-0147.md:5` gives it to a registrar; the body at `:47–53` and the notebook at `:171` and `:179` all give it to him. **Not repaired in the chapter; recorded.**
- ⚠ **THERE IS NO MAN OF SIXTY-SIX IN VOLUME 03.** He is a **woman of sixty-six**, Priscilla Nunn of 14 Ander's Row, in ten places across Chapters 146 and 150, four in 146 and six in 150, and `state/continuity.md` and `state/open-threads.md` both recorded a man of sixty-six, and neither of them was in the prose. **The new sections of those files are corrected. The historical sections are not, because a record of a belief is not a repair of a fact and rewriting history is its own class of lie.**
- ⚠ **DOUGLAS NAIRN IS A MAN OF FORTY-FOUR.** `chapter-0144.md:45` calls him *a woman of forty-four* and `:49` names him; `chapter-0148.md:170` calls him a man of forty-three and `:7` calls him a man of forty-four. **Not repaired; recorded.**
- ⚠ **`Ada Iles` is not a person in this manuscript yet.** She is a row in `bible/characters.md`, and the row asserted that Volume 03's resolution says in a sentence that she committed a falsification which saved people and made later erasure harder to trace, **and no such sentence is in any of the fifty chapters.** **The row is struck and the strikethrough carries the finding, because a bible row that says a thing is on the page when it is not on the page is a false lock, and a false lock is the documented way this manuscript loses a correction.**

## THE OTHERS, ALL COMPETENT, NONE A VILLAIN, NONE A FOOL, AND NONE OF THEM ASKED

**Vera Pilling, 81** — the register's keeper, entry 141 on about the hundred and ninth page of about a hundred and fifty, about forty-one pages left, about four hundred more things to come, and a fourth line on the first page that is the only line in that book addressed to anybody. **She has never been asked a question in this case and she is not going to be, and the only words she heard on the first of October were two words from a man of thirty-two and neither of them was a question.** The only promotion in the manuscript. **A keeper is not a leader and no volume may give her one.**

**Sheila Kearney, 57** — the turn of Batch 0004 and the second book. An exercise book, sixty pence, no heading and no names, about two hundred households, growing at about ten a day, and a bad ankle. **Nobody may touch it, copy it, circulate it, advise against it or destroy it, and nobody may say no to it, and the second half is the harder one: if she says no then she stops, and if she stops then there is nothing.**

**Priscilla Nunn, 66, of 14 Ander's Row** — the turn of Batch 0005. A piano in a removals van, a sewing machine that belongs to her sister, a wedding hamper, seventeen hundred and forty pounds, a date in the fourth box saying **9 OR 10 DEC** in a hand that was doing its best, and four offices since July all of which were right. **She is the answer to the question the whole volume has been asking and she is not Elias Venn and she is not a registrar, and she is a woman, and a man of thirty-two had the sentence ready and did not say it.**

**Marguerite Sowle, 69, of 27 Ander's Row** — the living person the volume injured. Her name went into a list of the drowned at about 11:20 on Friday 3 September 2025 in a hall before about forty people, on Elias Venn's reading of a bounded thing, and it cannot be unsaid, and the card that admits it is `RG/26/6` and **the name on the card is Elias's.** She has been waiting eleven weeks to be asked about a door. **She is not a villain and she is not a device and what happened to her is the first time in this series that his power has injured a living person, and it was not supernatural, and it was a man believing a feeling.**

**W. Lockhart, about 24** — eleven weeks in post on 15 November 2025 and **fourteen** on 6 December 2025. Three lines in the day book and one of them undated. **He is not a god returning and not a bell sounding and not a door opening, and nobody asked him anything for the first eleven chapters of Volume 04, and a member of the public read what he wrote and did not ask him and is not going to.**

**Ivo Pell, 25 · Mara Iles, 28 · Hesta Rell, 61 · R. Iqbal, 47 · Y. Carrow, 48 · H. Cray, 58 · M. Cope, 34 · D. Nairn, 44 · D. Vassallo, 41 · C. Lyle, 50 · G. Petherick, about 55 · S. Kearney, 57 · P. Nunn, 66 · V. Pilling, 81 · H. Venn, 58 · A. Osei, 36 · Y. Petch, 51 · C. Prowse, 63 · W. Okoro, 52 · S. Molloy, 47 · C. Price, 29 · D. Morgan, 47 · M. Sowle, 69 · E. Sowerby, 91, of The Paddock, no memorial to be provided, family cannot** — see the relationships and the thread list. **Anja Ward is in the prose of Volume 03 and the house is quiet about that and the silence is not a gap. Ada Iles is not in the prose of Volume 03 at all and the house was not quiet about that and it should have been.**

## THE RELATIONSHIP DECISION THAT BELONGS TO VOLUME 04 AND IS RECORDED HERE SO IT CANNOT BE MOVED

**Volume 04 opens with the two of them who have not said it, working in the same room, and the word is said ONCE in the whole volume, in Movement 2, at about Chapter 164, by Mara Iles to Elias Venn, about whether a third person can be trusted to hold a floor ledger — about somebody's reliability and not about a declaration, and neither of them improves on it.** The series outline puts first reliable trust in V3–V4 and the volume that has to carry the word is Volume 04. **A volume that opens on the word opens on a thing that has not been earned on the page. A volume that never says it stalls the line. The word has to arrive, and it has to arrive attached to a question about somebody else's reliability, because that is the shape the word has in this city: it is used about documents, and about people, late, and by somebody else, and about something small.**
