Six new working people are written out in full below and every one of them is competent, none is a villain and none is a fool.**

## THE RELATIONSHIPS, ONE LINE EACH, AND NONE OF THEM IS SETTLED, AND THE WORD IS STILL NOT SAID

- **Elias / Mara — first reliable in practice, eleven metres, and neither of them said anything about it.** `trust(ed|ing|y)?` returns **0** across the ten chapters, and the word is said once in this volume, in Movement 2, at about Chapter 164, by her, about whether a third person can be trusted to hold a floor ledger. **What she did in this batch: she walked a man with no post up a controlled stair and logged one card event and wrote him down as her guest in her own book and not in a minute, because a minute is read out to a person who was not in the room; she asked a housing officer of fifty-eight a question nobody in the case had ever put to her and said out loud, before he spoke, that she was asking it and he was not; she read a number a woman of sixty-seven had carried for sixteen years in a room with four people in it and did not minute it and did not put it in her own book; she told a woman of seventy-four the truth about eleven years in a telephone call of two minutes and forty seconds and said *I am the person who telling you* and *nobody is going to say that is the right length of time for a promise*; and she was about eleven metres from a man holding a box of labels on 3 January and did not mention the November finding that the case can do its work without him in about four days, and he noticed, and neither of them said anything, and both halves of that are on the page.** What he paid: a man of twenty-four he did not ask, twice; a number he could not check; a mask that gave him nothing; **and the reaching, twice in one week, in a housing office and a corridor, with the reason given in about four seconds both times and a registrar's improvement on it that the improvement was in the direction of less.**
- **Elias / Helen Venn — she is his mother, she is fifty-eight, and she is not in this batch.** She is the reason he did not ask a man of twenty-four on 15 November and she is the fourth reason on 1 January, and **in this batch the fourth reason stopped being a reason and became a habit: he noticed that he has started writing down the other three and not the fourth, that writing three is a way of not writing one, and that noticing that is the fourth reason, and that it has been the fourth reason since November and he did not notice it then.** She still owns the only instrument that can check him and it has found nothing wrong and cannot find anything wrong, and this batch did not use her once.
- **Elias / Ivo Pell, 25 — the folder, and the thing that has now happened to somebody else.** The note on the corner of the folder, dated 29 October 2025 and not opened until 2031: *a case which keeps its memory in one person has a single point of failure and it does not look like a break, it looks like a competent man.* **He telephoned on 5 December and said one sentence, that the new man at the front desk was on at about ten on a Monday and he was not, and that is how the man of thirty-two came to be in that building on the volume's first day. He is not in the ten chapters and he is not party to anything and he keeps a note anyway.** In Chapter 158 the man of thirty-two observed that the person who had actually been keeping the memory in one person for seventeen years was a woman of sixty-seven, and that he himself had wanted, for about a fortnight, to be the only one, and did not want it because it would be exactly what is written on the corner of a folder he has not opened.
- **Elias / Menna Prothero, 52 — the volume's moral centre in a professional's mouth, and the only new relationship in this batch that is load-bearing.** Nineteen years on the fourth level. She writes the labels, she wrote the rule in about 1998 that is four lines long and has never been printed and never been minuted, she keeps about forty filter cartridges in a bag in the label room, she holds the key to the drawer with the forty-eight books on her own belt, she put the 1978 ledger beside the 2009 ledger in a corridor and said a hand that does not change over thirty-one years is a fast hand and not a careful one, she read two cut stubs and refused to put a year on them, she said the answer to the volume's question in about two seconds and gave it again in about two seconds, **and she said the labels are the only thing keeping about nine people safe in four words and then said she is not going to add to it and will answer in a room if she is asked in a room and is prepared to hear the answer.** She is not a villain, she is not wrong, and the reason the volume is what it is going to be is a sentence she said in a corridor on 9 December. **He asked her nothing she had not already asked herself and she gave him four things and a second reading of them in the order he read them in, and he could not check that order, and she said she was a woman who has been on a floor nineteen years and knew exactly what a person sounds like when they are telling the truth about the shape of a hole, and she did not ask him one question about it.**
- **Elias / Hazel Cray, 58, housing officer, twenty-two years — the term, and the sentence of the week, and neither of them improved on anybody.** She answered the question nobody in the case had ever put to her and said nothing happened on the day it expired, and gave the two letters, and the drawer, and the form with three boxes and no box for a household, and said she is not going to be the person who puts seven households into it in December and is not going to be talked out of it and offered to have it minuted and signed. **She said the reason nobody has written again in nine words — a reissue makes somebody's paper out of date — and a registrar of twenty-eight said that is the third time that sentence has been the answer in this case and the second time she has given it, and she had never once been the only person in the room who has said it.** She also said the second clause of the Cauldwell arrangement is the only reason any of it was possible and that she wrote it and would write it again and has not written again, and that she has not asked a Director a question since the seventh of October.
- **Elias / Rosalind Peake, 74 — the first time anybody in this case has spoken to her, and she was owed eleven letters.** A first-floor flat on Corrie's Row, Northglass. A bequest of 11 September 2014 with a clause in a will of 2 March 2011 in her own hand about being told once a year, and a column on a file in a corridor that has been empty since 2014. **She said she had not been angry about it for about nine years and had been something else since 2016, which is being a person who gave a thing away and then became a person who does not know where it is, and she said that is a different thing from being cheated and she had not had anybody to say that to until a registrar of twenty-eight said it in nine words at about four minutes past four on 4 January 2026.** She asked whether she could come and see it and was told that she can, and was told there is no charge and no form and she does not have to tell anybody she came. **She does not know why a floor matters, and she is not going to be told in a telephone call.**
- **Elias / Beryl Nancarrow, 67 — one telephone call, one room, one number, and one refusal at a door.** Museum paper conservator 1978 to 2009, thirty-one years, and she wrote the fourth-level floor ledger in her own hand for every one of them. **She telephoned the main number on 27 December and would not say the number on a telephone, and she came on the twenty-eighth on a Tuesday because there is a bus on a Tuesday and there is not one on a Monday, and she said she did not come on a Tuesday for the case.** She gave one hundred and four in a room with four people in it, and a registrar wrote it down and did not minute it. **The reason she gave for a room and not a telephone is about a person and not about a document, and it is the best answer anybody has given this case in sixteen years: a number in a person's head is worth nothing on its own and is worth something the second there is a second person in the room who heard it, and she had a pen out on the bus, and what stopped her was that she would have been the only person who had it.** At the door she was told that the case has found something and that it is not going to tell her what and that she is welcome to ask, and she said then I will not ask, and asked for it written down that she was not asked and that she did not ask, **and said there is a difference between those two things and it is the only difference there has ever been in this building.** She is not a villain, not a device, and she confessed to nothing, and nobody in this batch asked her what happened in 2009.
- **Elias / W. Lockhart, about 24 — nineteen weeks and one day in post on 4 January 2026, and a person.** He is the volume's opening image and he is not an omen. **Elias Venn has read that day book standing up twice, on 15 November 2025 and on 1 January 2026, and the first time was a decision and the second time was a decision, and he did not read it at all in Chapter 151, and the reason for that is on the page and it is the reason Chapter 159's *this is the second time* is true.** He has a bus, a sandwich, a card with a photograph on it from August, a third night of four, and three lines in a book anybody may read. He has never been asked anything. **He does not know who is putting him on the nights, he asked twice and was told there was no rota, he sent an email to a duty address and got three lines back of which two were about his hours and one was a link, and nobody in this case has asked him about the link.**
- **Elias / the man of twenty-four is not the only person on a rota that does not exist, and the escalation is the number and not the size.** One night relief guard · about nine people who work the fourth level · about forty people who work the Museum's nights, which is **thirty-eight** and the thirty-eight is the figure · about nine people who come up to the fourth level, whose objects carry the labels, who are alive and legally uncountable. **And the thing that cannot be given a number at all is the district: about nineteen hundred tenancies, and no office in this city can say how many people are behind them.**
- **Elias / Anja Ward, 53 — eleven weeks and one day without a question, and not in this batch, and not told about a bequest clause.** She is the hand on the only instrument that closes the fourth level, in May 2026, in two capacities, on a real gas risk, and she is not told anything in these ten chapters and knows nothing about a district and nothing about a woman of seventy-four and nothing about a registrar who can do without him.

## THE NEW WORKING PEOPLE, ALL COMPETENT, NONE A VILLAIN, NONE A FOOL, AND NONE OF THEM ASKED

- **Dilys Kernan, 49** — at the front desk of the Morrowgate Museum of Quiet Saints for twenty-six years, nineteen of them in that room. **She read the three lines in the day book and did not ask the man about them and was not going to.** She told a member of the public on 6 December 2025 that he was the fourth person that month who had said he was not here about anything and that two of them were telling the truth. She told him, without being asked, what is in the book, and that she said nothing and said goodnight and put his card on the counter, and that he asked her at about half past six in the morning whether the third column was still in use and she said it was not and he wrote that down in the book, which he should not have done, and she did not tell him not to, **because there is no form for it and he is the only person on nights.** She keeps the front-desk duty list and it is current.
- **Bronwen Tarrant, 36** — four years in the security office, out of a hospital, no patience for a person who wants a story. **She gave the four reasons a card history is altered, with four fingers up, and said a log is written by a door and a door does not know a person, and that looking for an edit means asking why, and that she has done the one that is a person about twice in four years and both times it was a man who went home without his card and rang her from a bus stop.** She said there is no rota for nights in that building, that she has looked, that she is not the only person who has looked, that she asked once in writing and got a reply saying the night establishment is not scheduled as a roster because the establishment is casual, and that the establishment is not the question: *the establishment is thirty-eight people and I could give you that number in about four seconds and it would not tell you one single thing about who is on tonight, and that is not because the number is wrong, it is because a person is not a post.*
- **Rhiannon Iddon, 44** — nineteen years of this Museum's accession record, keeper of the quarterly binder, signatory of about four hundred appointment forms she could not tell you one of now. **She found the two cut leaves with her thumb in about four seconds, established that a description with a hole in it is a different thing from a name with a hole in it, read out the page of the quarterly binder twice because nobody wrote the first reading down, said that a file is never retired and a file is opened when a person is appointed and that there is no process in this city by which a file exists for a person who was never appointed, and said that nothing has been destroyed and that this is not a hole somebody made, it is a shape that nothing was ever put into.** She also said the day rota is four years out of date in August every year and everybody knows it and nobody has written to anybody.
- **Hilary Voss, 51** — nine years running the casual establishment for the nights. **She built a wage schedule from hours submitted by a floor, signed it, and never met the man whose name was on it and does not know whether he worked here.** She took the name off a telephone in about 2018, did not take a reference because there is no box on the form for one and there has never been one, has not had the piece of A4 since about 2019 and never will, and said so out loud before anybody could put it to her. **She said she is not going to let anybody make her into the person who let a man disappear out of a building, and she is not going to resign over it and is not going to be asked to, and that she had never once in nine years thought to go and look at a ring binder on the second level to see whether a name on her own wage schedule was a person the building had appointed, and she asked for that to be written down.** She said the thirty-eight are fine and about nine of them are on a night and every one of them is paid.
- **A conservator of about thirty-four** — took the telephone call on 27 December and wrote it on a pad in the order it happened because she was told to put anything through to a registrar, and did not write down the reason a woman gave for not saying a number on a telephone because she was writing the call down, and has thought about that since. Did the stock count on 23, 27, 28 and 30 December **because a stock count is not affected by a statutory break, and the only reason it is not affected is that nobody has ever argued that it should be.** Was thanked in a corridor in about eleven seconds by a woman of sixty-seven and it was not written down.
- **A man of about thirty-one** — does the collections survey on that floor, is in on Wednesdays, and **has never once been asked a question by anybody in this case.** He is named in Chapter 152 and in no chapter's notebook and that is a fact about the batch and not about him.

- ⚠ **Lockhart's tenure was nineteen weeks in two chapters and is eighteen weeks and one day on 4 January 2026 and seventeen weeks and five days on 1 January 2026**, thirty August 2025 being his start, derived twice and now in this state layer. Repaired in both, and Chapter 151 now shows the derivation in days.
- ⚠ **The length of this case was given as twenty-five weeks in thirteen places and that figure is Volume 03 Chapter 103's protagonist clock, and the case's own clock in this batch is fifty-two to fifty-six weeks off the sixth of December 2024 and thirty-one weeks off the first of June 2025.** All thirteen rewritten to anchor dates. **And `chapter-0150.md:100`'s twenty-one weeks for the registrar of twenty-eight is logged and not repaired.**
- ⚠ **Beryl Nancarrow's page-count was seventeen years old in eight places and it is sixteen years and about eight months, because the batch dated it to April 2009.** Repaired in three chapters, and the discrepancy is on the page in Chapter 158. ⚠ **CORRECTED 2026-09-26: the count was *eight places in the prose and the state layer*, and the discrepancy is with FIVE files and not four — `outline/volume-04.md`, `workspace/volume-04/batch-0001/PROMPT.md`, `state/character-state.md`, `state/continuity.md` and `state/open-threads.md`, eleven occurrences in all and none in `chapters/`. `bible/characters.md` was named in an earlier draft of this line and does not carry the figure.**
- ⚠ **The word *trust* appeared once, in Chapter 160's notebook, about checking the panel's age.** Repaired, because the word is said once in this volume in Movement 2 and a batch that uses it for arithmetic spends it.
- ⚠ **The phrase *eleven weeks* appeared three times on three unrelated intervals and could have been greppable as the figure for the 2011 instruction's unanswered question.** All three rewritten into days and dates. **`eleven weeks` and `twenty-five weeks` now both return zero across the ten chapters.**
- **Eleven notebook blockquote lines carried a stray closing bold marker** — a line that opened with a bold sentence and ran on bold to the end of the line, and so had three `**` and one unopened run. **Found by the per-line parity instrument that strips code spans only, and repaired in the files, and the instrument now returns 0 odd lines and 0 four-asterisk sequences across all ten.**

## THE RELATIONSHIP DECISION THAT BELONGS TO MOVEMENT 2 AND IS RECORDED HERE SO IT CANNOT BE MOVED

**The word is said ONCE in the whole of Volume 04, in Movement 2, at about Chapter 164, by Mara Iles to Elias Venn, about whether a third person can be trusted to hold a floor ledger — about somebody's reliability and not about a declaration, and neither of them improves on it. `trust(ed|ing|y)?` returns 0 across Chapters 151 to 160 and this batch printed it zero times on purpose, having found it once in its own draft and taken it out.** The reasoning is the reasoning of the whole manuscript: **the series outline puts first reliable trust in V3 to V4, and Volume 03 closed on a registrar who can do without him in four days and a man who had a sentence ready and did not say it, and a volume that opens on the word opens on a thing that has not been earned on the page, and a volume that never says it stalls the line. The word has to arrive, and it has to arrive attached to a question about somebody else's reliability, because that is the shape the word has in this city: it is used about documents, and about people, late, and by somebody else, and about something small.**

## THE FIGURES AND THE HOURS THAT THE INDEPENDENT REVIEW OF 2026-09-26 CHANGED, SO THAT A LATER BATCH DOES NOT CARRY THE FIRST DRAFT

- ⚠ **Beryl Nancarrow's number is one hundred and four, and it is a count of the leaves she wrote in and not a count of the book.** The first draft's 231, with its two cut leaves at 206 and 217 dated the second and thirteenth of April 2009, **could not be true at any rate: one line to a leaf for every night of the year puts 2 April 2009 on leaf 92 and 14 April on leaf 104, and 92 is a locked numeral.** **The two cut leaves are at ninety-one and one hundred and two, on the first and the twelfth of April 2009, and the hundred and fourth leaf is the fourteenth of April, which is the day she stopped, and the chapter says on the page that the two numbers are derived off the first of January and are not chosen.** She is still sixty-seven, still the paper conservator from 1978 to 2009, still thirty-one years, still not a villain and still not asked what she did. ⚠ **And she still leaves at about half past eleven, not about twenty past — the first draft had her spoken to at the door ten minutes after she had already gone.**
- ⚠ **Ivor Trask's file does not exist because the shelf holds fifty-two ring binders, one a year from 1974, every year present, and not twenty-nine.** Twenty-nine would have ended the run in 2002 and the 2019 binder the same scene opens could not have been on the shelf. He is fifty-eight, born the eleventh of June 1967, on a payroll form's box for a date of birth, and about nine payroll lines for a run in 2019 with the same hours on every one, and there is no `PF` file and no appointment form and no gap in the run, and **the whole of it is a shape that nothing was ever put into.** ⚠ **The 2019 binder has about two hundred and ten appointment forms in it, which is about fifty-three pages at four forms to a page, because a page holds twenty-eight ruled lines and a form is seven of them.** Hilary Voss is still fifty-one and still has never met him.
- ⚠ **W. Lockhart, 24, is on the hours his own day book carries and the batch had him in the wrong ones.** `NIGHT. 22:10 – 06:00`, card 1188 issued 21:40, arrival at about ten to ten **at night**, and a sandwich at that desk at about half past midnight on about ninety of the nights he has worked. **The second reading of the day book is now on the night of Saturday 1 January 2026, in a lobby whose galleries shut at five and whose lobby does not, and the chapter's own sentence is restored to something true: in November the man was a line in a book, and in January he is a man at a desk, and about half past midnight he is a man at that same desk with a sandwich in his hand.** Dilys Kernan is still forty-nine and is still at that desk and has still not asked him anything.
- ⚠ **Menna Prothero, 52, is still the curator of the Quiet Wing and is still not a villain, and the ledger she kept is still not on the schedule, and the two leaves are still not hers in this batch's telling.** ⚠ **But `TG/09/` is no longer *written in to the last leaf*:** it is the same size as the other forty-seven and it is written in to the hundred and fourth leaf with nothing on any of the leaves after that, **and a book that is a quarter full is a book somebody put down in April and not a book that came to an end, and the chapter now says both sentences and keeps them apart.** She still declines to put a year on the cut and still says nothing about it is fresh.
- ⚠ **Mara Iles, 28, is still a registrar and still the person who asks and says out loud that she is asking.** ⚠ **In Chapter 160 the firm of solicitors is on the third floor of a building on Lantern Row and not on a hill, and the chapter says the street out loud and says that a society in this city takes its name from that street and has nothing to do with the firm.** And the letter to the executor went on the Monday by ordinary post and the woman of seventy-four was telephoned on the Tuesday, **and the chapter now says on the page that the letter is not why she was telephoned, that a telephone number on a bequest file from 2014 is the reason, and that there is no form in this city that says whether a number from 2014 is a number anybody in this case may ring, and that she rang it and did not look for the form first.**
- ⚠ **THE `BQ` BEQUEST IS THE KETTERING BEQUEST.** The first draft opened the series in 1968 *out of the Ashgill bequest*, and **`Ashgill` is 14 Ashgill Row, Bankfoot, and the woman of about forty-three, and it is reserved and may not be used for anything else in this manuscript.** A proper noun is the cheapest thing in a novel to collide with and the most expensive to unpick four volumes later.
